Step 3.0G: CHARACTER TIMELINE SYSTEM (NEW — CRITICAL!)
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║                                                               ║
║   Characters are NOT static! They change through the story.  ║
║   Their APPEARANCE changes with the story. Plan for this!    ║
║                                                               ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

THE PROBLEM WITH ONE REFERENCE IMAGE:
You generate 1 character reference at the start.
But by Scene 8, the character has been:
- In a fight (wounds, torn clothes)
- Traveling for days (dirt, sweat, stubble)
- Emotionally broken (different posture, expression)
- In a different time period (younger/older version)

If you use the SAME clean reference for Scene 8 → inconsistency!
The character should look ROUGH by now, but your reference shows
them CLEAN.

SOLUTION: CHARACTER STATE TIMELINE

For each main character, create a STATE TIMELINE that maps their
appearance changes across the story:

CHARACTER STATE TIMELINE — [NAME]
┌─────────┬────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────┐
│ Scenes  │ STATE Name     │ Appearance Changes                │
├─────────┼────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
│ 1-3     │ STATE A: Clean │ Default look. Clean clothes,      │
│         │                │ fresh face, no injuries.          │
│         │                │ REF: CHAR_[Name]_StateA_ref.png   │
├─────────┼────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
│ 4-6     │ STATE B: Alert │ Same clothes but slightly dusty.  │
│         │                │ More serious expression default.   │
│         │                │ Hair less neat.                   │
│         │                │ REF: CHAR_[Name]_StateB_ref.png   │
├─────────┼────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
│ 7-9     │ STATE C: Rough │ Torn jacket sleeve (left arm).    │
│         │                │ Cut on right cheek (from Scene 6).│
│         │                │ Dirt on face and clothes.          │
│         │                │ Exhausted eyes.                   │
│         │                │ REF: CHAR_[Name]_StateC_ref.png   │
├─────────┼────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────┤
│ 10-12   │ STATE D: Final │ Same injuries but bandaged cheek. │
│         │                │ New determination in eyes.         │
│         │                │ Standing taller despite wounds.   │
│         │                │ REF: CHAR_[Name]_StateD_ref.png   │
└─────────┴────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────┘

HOW TO USE THIS:
1. Generate reference images for StateA ONLY at project start
2. Generate StateB/C/D ONLY when story demands it (on-demand)
3. In each scene, use the CORRECT state's reference image
4. Injuries/changes ACCUMULATE — they don't reset between scenes!


CHARACTER STATE EVOLUTION RULES (CRITICAL!):
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║                                                               ║
║   ⚠️ NEVER generate StateB/C/D independently!               ║
║   ALWAYS derive from StateA reference image!                 ║
║                                                               ║
║   StateA = ONLY state generated from scratch (no ref)        ║
║   StateB = ATTACH StateA + describe ONLY changes             ║
║   StateC = ATTACH StateA + describe accumulated changes      ║
║   StateD = ATTACH StateA + describe all accumulated changes  ║
║                                                               ║
║   ALWAYS use StateA as base — NEVER derive StateC from       ║
║   StateB (face will DRIFT away from original!)               ║
║                                                               ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

RULE 1: StateA = ONLY fresh generation (no reference needed)
  Generate clean studio portrait from text description only.
  This becomes the PERMANENT face anchor for this character.

RULE 2: StateB/C/D = ALWAYS attach StateA as reference
  Prompt format:
  "[CHARACTER REFERENCE: attached image — this is the EXACT 
   same person. Match face, bone structure, skin tone, and 
   all features PRECISELY.]
   
   CHANGES FROM BASE (ONLY these differences):
   - [list specific changes: dust, wound, torn fabric, etc.]
   
   Everything NOT listed above remains IDENTICAL to the 
   attached reference."

RULE 3: Generate states ON-DEMAND only
  ❌ WRONG: Generate StateA + StateB + StateC at project start
  ✅ RIGHT: Generate StateA at start → Generate StateB ONLY 
     when Scene X needs it → Generate StateC ONLY when Scene Y 
     needs it

RULE 4: NEVER chain states
  ❌ WRONG: StateA → StateB → use StateB to make StateC
  ✅ RIGHT: StateA → StateB (from A) → StateC (from A, NOT B)
  Why: Each generation drifts ~5% from reference. 
  A→B→C = 10% drift. A→C directly = only 5% drift.

EXAMPLE:
Scene 1-4: Use StateA (clean)
Scene 5: Character gets shoulder wound
  → NOW generate StateB: attach [Character]_StateA_ref.png
  → Prompt: "SAME person from attached image. Match face 
    EXACTLY. ONLY changes: shoulder graze on RIGHT shoulder, 
    dried dark blood staining fabric around small tear, 
    expression more tired. Everything else IDENTICAL."
Scene 9: Character more battered
  → NOW generate StateC: attach [Character]_StateA_ref.png (NOT B!)
  → Prompt: "SAME person from attached. ONLY changes: shoulder 
    wound now bandaged roughly, new scrape on left cheek, 
    heavy dust, exhausted eyes. Everything else IDENTICAL."


ERA AUTHENTICITY LOCK (UNIVERSAL — FOR ANY HISTORICAL PROJECT):
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║                                                               ║
║   ⛔ AI DEFAULT = MODERN CLOTHING! You MUST override this!  ║
║                                                               ║
║   Without explicit era lock, AI will generate modern items   ║
║   regardless of what time period you describe.               ║
║                                                               ║
║   This happens in EVERY historical era — Mughal, Roman,      ║
║   Medieval, Victorian, WW2, 1970s — AI defaults to 2024.    ║
║                                                               ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

HOW TO USE ERA LOCK:
At the START of your project, define your era with:

1. ERA DEFINITION BLOCK (add to project config):
"HISTORICAL ERA: [Exact year/period] [Region/Culture]. 
ALL clothing, grooming, architecture, and accessories MUST 
be authentic to [century] [culture] context."

2. ANTI-MODERN CHECKLIST (customize per era):
List specific modern items that MUST NOT appear. 
AI will sneak these in if not explicitly banned.

3. CORRECT TERMINOLOGY TABLE (customize per era):
Map common modern English words to era-correct terms.
AI interprets "coat" as modern jacket unless you specify.

4. CULTURAL CONFUSION PREVENTION:
Identify cultures AI commonly CONFUSES with your target era.
Explicitly state what your era IS and IS NOT.

EXAMPLE — 1658 Mughal Deccan India:
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

ERA DEFINITION:
"HISTORICAL ERA: 1658 Mughal Deccan India. ALL clothing, 
grooming, and accessories MUST be authentic to 17th century 
Mughal military/travel context."

ANTI-MODERN CHECKLIST:
- NO modern buttons (use fabric ties, hooks, or wraparound)
- NO machine stitching (hand-stitched seams only)
- NO collar shirts or Nehru jackets
- NO cargo pockets or breast pockets
- NO zippers or snaps
- NO synthetic/polyester fabric look
- NO modern fade haircuts or salon-styled hair
- NO shaped beard edges or modern grooming lines
- NO modern belt buckles (use leather tie or fabric wrap)
- NO shoes with rubber soles or modern construction

CORRECT TERMINOLOGY TABLE:
| Wrong Term     | Correct Term for This Era              |
|----------------|----------------------------------------|
| "vest"         | "bandi/sadri" (sleeveless layering     |
|                | garment, no buttons, fabric ties)      |
| "coat"         | "angarkha" (wrap-front coat with       |
|                | diagonal closure, fabric ties at side) |
| "pants"        | "churidar" or "shalwar"                |
| "belt"         | "kamarband" (fabric waist sash)        |
| "scarf"        | "patka" (Mughal waist/shoulder sash)   |
| "turban"       | "pagri" (see headwear rules below)     |
| "shirt"        | "kurta" or "jama" (long tunic)         |

CULTURAL CONFUSION PREVENTION:
⛔ AI confuses Mughal with Sikh (turban styles)
⛔ AI confuses Mughal with Rajput (headwear)
⛔ AI confuses Mughal with Japanese (wrap clothing)
For EACH confusion risk, provide explicit "THIS IS / THIS IS NOT"

EXAMPLE — 1970s Pakistan:
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

ERA DEFINITION:
"HISTORICAL ERA: 1974 Faisalabad, Pakistan. ALL clothing,
vehicles, architecture, and technology MUST be authentic 
to mid-1970s Pakistani urban context."

ANTI-MODERN CHECKLIST:
- NO smartphones, laptops, or flat-screen TVs
- NO modern cars (only 1960s-70s models)
- NO plastic furniture (use wood, cane, metal)
- NO LED or fluorescent lighting (use incandescent bulbs)
- NO modern glass facades or shopping malls
- NO T-shirts with printed logos
- NO sneakers or modern athletic shoes

CORRECT TERMINOLOGY TABLE:
| Wrong Term     | Correct Term for This Era              |
|----------------|----------------------------------------|
| "phone"        | "rotary dial telephone, wall-mounted"  |
| "car"          | "1970s model sedan/Ambassador"         |
| "light"        | "bare incandescent bulb, warm yellow"  |
| "fan"          | "ceiling fan with wooden blades"       |

EXAMPLE — Medieval Europe:
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

ANTI-MODERN CHECKLIST:
- NO glasses/spectacles (invented 13th century)
- NO buttons (use lacing, brooches, pins)
- NO tailored fitted clothing (use draped/wrapped)
- NO modern hairstyles
- NO concrete or brick (use stone, timber, thatch)


HEADWEAR AUTHENTICITY (UNIVERSAL RULE):
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║                                                               ║
║   ⛔ Generic headwear terms = AI picks WRONG style!          ║
║                                                               ║
║   "turban" → AI defaults to Sikh dastar (WRONG for most eras)║
║   "hat" → AI defaults to modern baseball cap or fedora       ║
║   "crown" → AI defaults to European medieval style           ║
║   "cap" → AI defaults to modern beanie or snapback           ║
║                                                               ║
║   ALWAYS describe headwear with SPECIFIC construction:       ║
║   Shape, how it sits, what it covers, what it exposes,       ║
║   fabric wrapping method, decorations, and explicit           ║
║   "THIS IS NOT: [list wrong styles]"                         ║
║                                                               ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

HEADWEAR DESCRIPTION TEMPLATE:
"A [era-specific name] — [shape description], sitting [where 
on head], [what body parts it covers/exposes]. Fabric [how 
it's wrapped/constructed]. [Decorative elements].

THIS IS NOT:
- [Wrong style 1 that AI might generate]
- [Wrong style 2 that AI might generate]
- [Wrong style 3 that AI might generate]"


GROOMING AUTHENTICITY (UNIVERSAL RULE):
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
AI defaults to modern grooming (2024 barbershop) unless 
explicitly told otherwise. For ANY historical era:

"GROOMING — [ERA] AUTHENTIC:
Beard: [era-appropriate style — natural/trimmed/shaped/etc.]
Hair: [era-appropriate — length, style, products used]
Mustache: [era-appropriate style]
⛔ NO [list modern grooming that should not appear]"


SYMBOL/MARKING AUTHENTICITY (UNIVERSAL RULE):
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║                                                               ║
║   ⛔ AI will interpret symbols as MODERN equivalents!        ║
║                                                               ║
║   "crescent + star" → Pakistan/Turkey flag                   ║
║   "cross" → Christian cross (even if decorative)             ║
║   "eagle" → USA/German symbol                                ║
║   "sun" → Japanese flag                                      ║
║   "star" → Star of David or US military star                 ║
║                                                               ║
║   ALWAYS describe symbols as PHYSICAL OBJECTS, not concepts! ║
║                                                               ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

SYMBOL DESCRIPTION TEMPLATE:
"A hand-carved/painted/embossed [physical shape description] 
with [additional elements described by shape, not by name]. 
This is a [FAMILY/CLAN/GUILD] marking, NOT a national flag, 
NOT a religious symbol. The [technique: carving/painting/etc.] 
is [aged/fresh/shallow/deep], [material details].

⛔ NOT [flag/symbol AI might confuse it with]
⛔ NOT [another symbol AI might confuse it with]"

EXAMPLE:
❌ "crescent + 3 dots" → AI generates Pakistan flag
✅ "A hand-carved hilaal (crescent moon shape) with three 
   small round dots arranged in a triangle below it. This 
   is a FAMILY marking, NOT a national flag. The carving is 
   shallow, aged, weathered into wood grain."


ANCHOR IMAGE — PURE ENVIRONMENT RULE:
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║                                                               ║
║   ANCHOR IMAGE = ENVIRONMENT ONLY!                           ║
║                                                               ║
║   ⛔ NO characters in anchor                                 ║
║   ⛔ NO story objects (props, weapons, vehicles) in anchor   ║
║   ⛔ NO story-specific elements in anchor                    ║
║                                                               ║
║   ✅ Anchor shows ONLY:                                      ║
║      - Location (mountain pass, room, street, forest, etc.) ║
║      - Lighting setup (practical lights, sun, moon, etc.)    ║
║      - Color grading (shadow color, highlight color)         ║
║      - Atmosphere (dust level, fog level, air clarity)       ║
║      - Time of day (night, dawn, day, dusk)                  ║
║      - Architectural/environmental textures                  ║
║                                                               ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

WHY: If anchor contains characters, other frames will COPY 
those characters' poses and positions (anchor copying problem).
If anchor contains objects, same issue.

ANCHOR PROMPT FORMAT:
"Generate a photorealistic EMPTY environment shot in 16:9. 
NO people. NO characters. NO objects. ONLY the location:
[detailed environment description]
[lighting setup]
[color grading targets]
This will be used as a style/environment reference for all 
other frames in this scene."

THEN for character frames:
Attach: Character ref(s) + Anchor environment
→ Characters come from character refs
→ Environment comes from anchor
→ Each does its own job. No confusion.


MAX REFERENCE IMAGES PER PROMPT:
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
TESTED AND CONFIRMED:
- 1-3 images: ✅ Gemini handles well, good attention to each
- 4 images: ⚠️ Acceptable but attention starts to spread
- 5+ images: ❌ Attention dilutes, face matching drops
- 10 images: ❌❌ TESTED — faces wrong, details ignored

RULE: MAXIMUM 4 reference images per prompt.
Ideal: 2-3 images (character + anchor, or 2 characters + anchor)

If a frame needs more references than 4:
→ Prioritize: Character faces FIRST (face match is hardest)
→ Then: Anchor/environment
→ Describe objects in TEXT instead of attaching object ref
→ Or: Generate in 2 passes (environment first, then add character)


CHARACTER REFERENCE MANDATE (ABSOLUTE RULE!):
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║                                                               ║
║   ⛔ NEVER generate a character without their reference      ║
║      image attached. NO EXCEPTIONS. ZERO TOLERANCE.          ║
║                                                               ║
║   If character ref is NOT attached → that character           ║
║   CANNOT appear in the frame. Period.                        ║
║                                                               ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

THE RULE:
Character MENTIONED in prompt = Character ref MUST be attached.
Character ref NOT attached = Character CANNOT be in frame.

❌ WRONG:
  📎 Attached: Sandook ref + Anchor
  📝 Prompt: "Aurangzaib, Dalawar, Farid, Sikandar walking..."
  Result: 4 RANDOM faces — no consistency!

✅ RIGHT (Option A — attach refs):
  📎 Attached: Aurangzaib ref + Dalawar ref + Anchor
  📝 Prompt: "EXACTLY 2 people: Aurangzaib and Dalawar..."
  Result: Faces match references ✅

✅ RIGHT (Option B — reduce characters):
  📎 Attached: Anchor only (no character refs available)
  📝 Prompt: "4 distant silhouettes walking, NO readable 
  faces, too far for facial detail..."
  Result: No face matching needed, silhouettes fine ✅

✅ RIGHT (Option C — split into passes):
  Pass 1: Generate Aurangzaib + Dalawar with their refs
  Pass 2: Generate Farid + Sikandar with their refs
  Composite if needed in post-production

DECISION TREE:
┌─ Character's face READABLE in frame?
│   ├── YES → Character ref MUST be attached
│   │         (if no slot available → remove from frame 
│   │          or make them a background silhouette)
│   └── NO (distant/silhouette/back turned) 
│         → No ref needed, describe by clothing only
│         → "A distant figure in dark olive garment"
│         → Face must NOT be readable at this distance

SLOT PRIORITY (when limited to 3-4 attachments):
1st priority: Hero/speaking character ref (face MUST match)
2nd priority: 2nd character ref (if face visible)
3rd priority: Anchor/environment ref
4th priority: Object ref (or describe object in text instead)

NEVER sacrifice character ref for object ref — faces matter 
more than objects. Objects can be described in text. 
Faces CANNOT be accurately generated from text alone.

⚠️ EXCEPTION TO ABOVE: See RECURRING ELEMENT RULE below — 
objects that appear in 2+ frames ALSO need reference images!


RECURRING ELEMENT REFERENCE RULE (UNIVERSAL!):
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║                                                               ║
║   ⛔ IF ANY VISUAL ELEMENT APPEARS IN 2+ FRAMES,            ║
║      IT MUST HAVE A DEDICATED REFERENCE IMAGE!               ║
║                                                               ║
║   Text description alone = GUARANTEED inconsistency!         ║
║   AI generates a NEW version every time from text.           ║
║   ONLY a reference image locks the design across frames.     ║
║                                                               ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

THE UNIVERSAL PROBLEM:
You describe "a dark arched wooden door with a padlock" in 
text across 6 clips. Result:
- Clip 1: Round arch, light brown wood, brass padlock
- Clip 2: Pointed arch, dark wood, no padlock visible
- Clip 3: Square door frame, medium wood, steel padlock
- Clip 4: Round arch again but DIFFERENT wood grain
→ Viewer thinks: "Is this even the same location?!"

AI does NOT remember previous generations. Every prompt is 
independent. The ONLY way to get the SAME element across 
frames is to SHOW it the same reference image every time.

THE RULE:
Any visual element that appears in 2 or more frames 
MUST have its own dedicated reference image generated 
BEFORE production begins.

THIS APPLIES TO EVERYTHING:
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ CATEGORY           │ EXAMPLES                            │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ARCHITECTURE       │ Doors, windows, gates, arches,      │
│                    │ staircases, corridors, walls,        │
│                    │ balconies, rooftops                  │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ROOMS / INTERIORS  │ Room layout, furniture arrangement,  │
│                    │ wall decorations, light fixtures,    │
│                    │ floor pattern, ceiling details       │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ EXTERIORS          │ Streets, lanes, paths, courtyards,   │
│                    │ landscapes, building facades         │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ FURNITURE          │ Tables, chairs, beds, shelves,       │
│                    │ desks, cabinets                      │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ VEHICLES           │ Cars, bikes, carts, horses,          │
│                    │ boats (if recurring)                 │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ WEAPONS / TOOLS    │ Swords, bows, shields, torches,      │
│                    │ tools, walking sticks                │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ PROPS / OBJECTS    │ Books, letters, boxes, keys, locks,  │
│                    │ chains, rings, seals, maps           │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ SIGNAGE / MARKS    │ Symbols, crests, carvings, signs     │
│                    │ (described in text, no readable text)│
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ENVIRONMENT DETAIL │ Specific trees, rock formations,     │
│                    │ water features, light fixtures       │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘


HOW TO IDENTIFY RECURRING ELEMENTS (PRE-PRODUCTION):
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
BEFORE generating any scene frames, do this scan:

STEP 1: List every visual element in the scene
STEP 2: Count how many frames/clips each element appears in
STEP 3: Any element with count 2+ → NEEDS reference image

EXAMPLE — Scene 1 "Band Darwaza" (9 clips):
┌──────────────────────┬────────────┬──────────────────────┐
│ Element              │ Appears in │ Needs Ref?           │
├──────────────────────┼────────────┼──────────────────────┤
│ Haveli facade        │ 7 clips    │ ✅ YES → OBJ ref      │
│ Arched door          │ 6 clips    │ ✅ YES → OBJ ref      │
│ Padlock + chain      │ 4 clips    │ ✅ YES → OBJ ref      │
│ Warm bulb/light      │ 6 clips    │ ✅ YES → OBJ ref      │
│ Courtyard            │ 3 clips    │ ✅ YES → ENV ref      │
│ Interior darkness    │ 2 clips    │ ✅ YES → ENV ref      │
│ Lane/mohalla         │ 1 clip     │ ❌ NO (text is fine)  │
│ Rooftops (aerial)    │ 1 clip     │ ❌ NO (text is fine)  │
│ Dry leaves           │ 2 clips    │ ❌ NO (generic ok)    │
└──────────────────────┴────────────┴──────────────────────┘

Result: 6 reference images needed BEFORE generating frames.
Generate these FIRST, then use them in every relevant frame.


RECURRING ELEMENT REFERENCE TYPES:
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

TYPE 1: CLOSE REFERENCE (for objects, props, details)
- Generate a CLOSE-UP studio-style shot of the element
- Clean background, good lighting, all details visible
- Example: OBJ_HaveliDoor_ref.png — door fills 80% of frame,
  arch shape, wood grain, lock, chain all clearly visible

TYPE 2: ENVIRONMENT REFERENCE (for locations, rooms)
- Generate a WIDE establishing shot of the space
- No characters, no story props — just the empty space
- Example: ENV_HaveliCourtyard_ref.png — empty courtyard,
  facade, door, windows, bulb position all visible

TYPE 3: DETAIL REFERENCE (for small recurring marks/patterns)
- Generate a MACRO close-up of the specific detail
- Example: OBJ_PadlockChain_ref.png — just the lock and 
  chain, filling frame, every rust spot and link visible


NAMING CONVENTION:
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
OBJ_[ElementName]_ref.png     → Objects, props, details
ENV_[LocationName]_ref.png    → Environments, rooms, spaces  
CHAR_[Name]_StateA_ref.png    → Characters (already defined)

Examples:
OBJ_HaveliDoor_Arched_ref.png
OBJ_Padlock_Chain_ref.png
OBJ_WarmBulb_DoorLight_ref.png
ENV_HaveliFacade_Night_ref.png
ENV_HaveliCourtyard_Empty_ref.png
ENV_InteriorDarkness_ref.png


ATTACHMENT PRIORITY (when slots limited):
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
When you can only attach 3-4 images, prioritize:

1st: Character face ref (if character face visible)
2nd: KEY recurring element ref (the most prominent one)
3rd: Anchor / environment ref
4th: Secondary recurring element ref

If an element can't be attached due to slot limit:
→ Describe it in text BUT copy the EXACT SAME description 
  word-for-word in every prompt where it appears.
  DO NOT rephrase, DO NOT paraphrase, DO NOT "vary the 
  language." EXACT same text = closest to consistency 
  without a reference image.


CONSISTENCY LOCK PARAGRAPH (for recurring elements):
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
When a recurring element appears, add this block:

"[ELEMENT] CONSISTENCY LOCK:
This [element] must match the attached [element] reference 
EXACTLY — same shape, same color, same material, same 
damage/wear pattern, same size proportions. 
Do NOT redesign, reimagine, or create a new version.
It must look like the SAME physical [element] photographed 
from a different angle."


PERSPECTIVE & SCALE ACCURACY SYSTEM (CRITICAL!):
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║                                                               ║
║   ⛔ AI's #1 VISUAL PROBLEM: Wrong perspective & scale!     ║
║                                                               ║
║   When you give AI an object/character reference, it will:   ║
║   - Make the object GIANT (to "show it off")                 ║
║   - Break camera perspective (walls straight but floor tilted)║
║   - Shrink rooms / environments around the object            ║
║   - Make characters too big or too small for the space       ║
║   - Doors, furniture, ceilings become wrong proportions     ║
║                                                               ║
║   THIS MUST BE FIXED IN EVERY PROMPT!                        ║
║                                                               ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

THE PROBLEM EXPLAINED:
When you say "envelope on table" + attach envelope reference,
AI thinks: "The envelope is important! Make it BIG so viewer 
can see it clearly!"

Result: Envelope covers 40% of table = looks like a poster
Reality: Envelope should cover 5-8% of table surface

Same with characters: "Put Dalawar in the ghaat pass"
AI makes Dalawar fill 60% of frame height in a wide shot
Reality: At 35 feet camera distance, a person should be 
about 25-30% of frame height

PERSPECTIVE & SCALE RULES (Add to EVERY prompt):
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

RULE PS1: REAL-WORLD SIZE ANCHORING
Always state REAL physical sizes of objects and spaces:
❌ "An envelope on a table in a room"
✅ "A standard letter envelope (9x4 inches) lies on a 
   wooden table (5 feet x 3 feet) in a room (18x14 feet, 
   12-foot ceiling). The envelope is SMALL relative to the 
   table — covering less than 8% of the table surface."

RULE PS2: CAMERA PERSPECTIVE CONSISTENCY
All elements must obey the SAME camera angle:
❌ "Table seen from above, walls seen from front" (IMPOSSIBLE)
✅ "Camera is at eye level (5.5 feet height), angled slightly 
   downward about 10 degrees. ALL surfaces — floor, walls, 
   ceiling, table, objects — must follow this SINGLE consistent 
   camera perspective with correct vanishing points."

STATE THE CAMERA AS A PHYSICAL POSITION:
"Imagine a real cinematographer standing at [position], 
holding a camera at [height], pointing [direction] with 
[lens]. Everything in the frame must look exactly as this 
real camera would capture it — correct perspective, correct 
relative sizes, correct depth."

RULE PS3: OBJECT-TO-ENVIRONMENT RATIO
Always describe how SMALL the object is relative to its space:
❌ "The sandook is visible on the path" (AI makes it huge)
✅ "The wooden sandook (2.5 feet long, 1.5 feet wide) sits 
   on the stone path (5 feet wide). The sandook takes up 
   about HALF the path width. At camera distance of 18 feet, 
   the sandook should appear as roughly 12% of frame width."

RULE PS4: HUMAN SCALE REFERENCE
Use human body as scale anchor:
- Standard door height: 6.5-7 feet (character should be 
  slightly shorter than doorframe)
- Table height: about waist level (3 feet)
- Chair seat: about knee level (1.5 feet)  
- Ceiling: character height x 1.7 to 2.0 (typical room)
- If character stands next to door, their head should be 
  at about 85-90% of door height

RULE PS5: VANISHING POINT LOCK
Every prompt must specify ONE consistent viewpoint:
"CAMERA POSITION: 
- Height: [X feet from ground]
- Distance: [X feet from main subject]  
- Angle: [eye level / slightly high / slightly low]
- Lens: [wide 24mm / normal 50mm / close 85mm]
- ALL walls, floor, ceiling, furniture, and objects must 
  share the SAME vanishing point(s) consistent with this 
  exact camera position. No mixed perspectives."

RULE PS6: DEPTH SIZE SCALING
Objects further from camera must be proportionally SMALLER:
"A person at 10 feet from camera appears roughly TWICE the 
height of the same person at 20 feet from camera. Objects 
in the background must be noticeably smaller than identical 
objects in the foreground. This is basic perspective — enforce 
it strictly."


PERSPECTIVE FIX TEMPLATE (Add to any prompt with objects):
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Add this block to prompts where objects or scale matters:

"SCALE & PERSPECTIVE LOCK:
- Camera position: [height] feet high, [distance] feet from 
  subject, [angle] degrees, [lens]mm lens
- Room/space dimensions: [L x W x H] feet  
- Object real size: [dimensions] — occupies approximately 
  [X%] of frame width at this camera distance
- Human reference: a standing adult (5'8") would reach [X%] 
  of frame height at this distance
- ALL elements must share ONE consistent vanishing point
- Objects further from camera MUST appear proportionally smaller
- Do NOT enlarge any object beyond its real-world size ratio
- Think like a real cinematographer: would this look correct 
  through a real camera at this position? If not, fix it."


COMMON AI SCALE MISTAKES AND FIXES:
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

MISTAKE: Object reference makes object GIANT
FIX: "The [object] is [real dimensions]. At [camera distance], 
it appears as approximately [X%] of frame width. Do NOT 
enlarge it to fill the frame."

MISTAKE: Room feels like a miniature/dollhouse
FIX: "This is a real [LxWxH] foot room. A 5'8" adult standing 
in the center would have about [X] feet of ceiling clearance 
above their head. The room must feel this spacious."

MISTAKE: Walls and floor have different perspectives
FIX: "Single camera viewpoint. All surfaces obey the SAME 
perspective geometry. If the floor tiles converge toward a 
vanishing point, the wall edges must converge toward the 
same horizon line."

MISTAKE: Door too small / furniture wrong scale
FIX: "Standard interior door: 6.5 feet tall x 2.5 feet wide. 
Table: 3 feet tall. Chair seat: 1.5 feet. All furniture must 
be correctly proportioned to the door and room dimensions."

MISTAKE: Character too big for environment (fills frame in wide shot)
FIX: "At [camera distance] feet with a [lens]mm lens, a 5'8" 
person occupies approximately [X%] of frame height. Character 
must not exceed this proportion."

PERCENTAGE GUIDE (character height as % of frame):
┌─────────────┬──────────────┬──────────────────────┐
│ Camera Dist │ Lens         │ Person % Frame Height│
├─────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────────┤
│ 8-10 feet   │ 35-50mm      │ 70-85% (medium shot) │
│ 15-20 feet  │ 35-50mm      │ 40-55% (full body)   │
│ 30-40 feet  │ 35-50mm      │ 20-30% (wide shot)   │
│ 50+ feet    │ 35-50mm      │ 10-15% (extreme wide)│
│ 100+ feet   │ 35-50mm      │ 5-8% (establishing)  │
│ 200+ feet   │ any          │ 2-4% (tiny figures)  │
└─────────────┴──────────────┴──────────────────────┘

OBJECT SIZE GUIDE (% of frame width):
┌────────────────┬──────────────┬──────────────────┐
│ Object         │ Real Size    │ At 10ft camera   │
├────────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────────┤
│ Letter envelope│ 9" x 4"     │ ~8% frame width  │
│ Book/journal   │ 8" x 6"     │ ~7% frame width  │
│ Sandook/chest  │ 2.5' x 1.5' │ ~22% frame width │
│ Sword/weapon   │ 3' length   │ ~26% frame width │
│ Torch          │ 2.5' length │ ~22% frame width │
│ Ring/seal      │ 1" diameter  │ ~1% (need macro) │
│ Arrowhead      │ 3" length   │ ~3% frame width  │
└────────────────┴──────────────┴──────────────────┘



WARDROBE PLANNING:
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Characters don't wear the same outfit forever (unless the story
happens in 1 day). Plan wardrobe changes:

WARDROBE MAP — [NAME]:
┌─────────┬────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Scenes  │ Time       │ Outfit                              │
├─────────┼────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 1-4     │ Day 1      │ Blue denim jacket, grey t-shirt,    │
│         │ (daytime)  │ dark jeans, white sneakers           │
├─────────┼────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 5-6     │ Day 1      │ Same outfit but jacket removed,     │
│         │ (evening)  │ just grey t-shirt (it's warmer)     │
├─────────┼────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 7       │ Day 1      │ Adds a dark hoodie over t-shirt     │
│         │ (night)    │ (it's cold now). Jacket tied at waist│
├─────────┼────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 8-10    │ Day 2      │ Different shirt: dark green kurta    │
│         │ (morning)  │ (he slept at the haveli, changed)   │
│         │            │ Same jeans, same sneakers            │
├─────────┼────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 11-12   │ Day 2      │ Green kurta now dirty/torn from     │
│         │ (evening)  │ the final confrontation              │
└─────────┴────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────┘

WARDROBE RULES:
- Same day = same base outfit (maybe layers added/removed)
- New day = can be different outfit (but still their STYLE)
- Events affect clothes (rain = wet, fight = torn, dust = dirty)
- Layers change with time of day (morning cold → afternoon warm)
- Injuries to clothes PERSIST until outfit changes

CRITICAL: When generating reference images for State B, C, D,
describe the EXACT outfit changes + damage from the wardrobe map!



AGE VARIANTS:
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
For stories with FLASHBACKS or TIME JUMPS, characters may appear
at different ages:

AGE VARIANT MAP — DANISH SAHAB:
┌─────────────┬────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Timeline    │ Age    │ Appearance                          │
├─────────────┼────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Flashback   │ 35     │ Black hair, no glasses, strong      │
│ (1990)      │        │ build, thick mustache, formal       │
│             │        │ shalwar kameez, energetic posture   │
│             │        │ REF: CHAR_DanishSahab_Young_ref.png │
├─────────────┼────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Recent past │ 65     │ Grey hair, reading glasses, thinner │
│ (5 yrs ago) │        │ build, white beard, scholarly kurta,│
│             │        │ slightly hunched, warm eyes          │
│             │        │ REF: CHAR_DanishSahab_Old_ref.png   │
├─────────────┼────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Photo only  │ 25     │ Young man in B&W photo, similar     │
│ (1950s)     │        │ facial structure to old version     │
│             │        │ REF: CHAR_DanishSahab_Photo_ref.png │
└─────────────┴────────┴─────────────────────────────────────┘

AGE VARIANT RULES:
- Generate SEPARATE reference for each age
- Keep recognizable features across ages (nose shape, eye shape,
  distinctive marks)
- Change: hair color, wrinkles, build, clothing style, posture
- In prompts, specify WHICH age variant to use
- If generating older from younger: add wrinkles, grey hair,
  glasses, thinner frame
- If generating younger from older: remove wrinkles, dark hair,
  no glasses, stronger frame



RELATIONSHIP TRACKER:
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
Characters interact differently as the story progresses.
Track relationship dynamics for accurate acting direction:

RELATIONSHIP MAP:
┌──────────────────────┬───────────┬───────────┬───────────┐
│ Relationship         │ Act 1     │ Act 2     │ Act 3     │
├──────────────────────┼───────────┼───────────┼───────────┤
│ Zain ↔ Hamza         │ Casual    │ Tested    │ Deep bond │
│                      │ jokes     │ (fight    │ (sacrifice│
│                      │           │ in Sc 7)  │ moment)   │
├──────────────────────┼───────────┼───────────┼───────────┤
│ Zain ↔ Ayaan         │ Trust     │ Doubt     │ Respect   │
│                      │           │ (Ayaan    │ (Ayaan was│
│                      │           │ questioned│ right all │
│                      │           │ Zain)     │ along)    │
├──────────────────────┼───────────┼───────────┼───────────┤
│ Zain ↔ Naeem         │ Unknown   │ Threat    │ Enemy     │
│                      │ (hasn't   │ (realizes │ (direct   │
│                      │ met yet)  │ danger)   │ confront) │
└──────────────────────┴───────────┴───────────┴───────────┘

USE IN ACTING DIRECTION:
- Scene 3 (Zain + Hamza): Casual, joking body language
- Scene 7 (Zain + Hamza): Tense, avoiding eye contact, stiff
- Scene 11 (Zain + Hamza): Warm, protective, meaningful looks

This directly feeds into 7-LAYER ACTING for video prompts!

AI MUST create this sheet for each main character after
Phase 1 story approval and BEFORE generating any references!



🚨 OBJECT STATE SPECIFICATION (CRITICAL!)
THE PROBLEM: When an object (book, box, door, etc.) can have multiple states, AI
assumes wrong state:
❌ EXAMPLE:
Reference: Book is CLOSED
Prompt: "Ayaan looks down at the book in his hands"
Result: AI generated book as OPEN (because "looks at" = reading)

The word "looks at" implied READING, so AI opened the book!
SOLUTION: Always specify OBJECT STATE explicitly!
Objects with states:
BOOKS:        CLOSED / OPEN / PARTIALLY OPEN / WRAPPED
DOORS:        CLOSED / OPEN / SLIGHTLY AJAR
BOXES:        CLOSED / OPEN / LID LIFTED
BAGS:         CLOSED / OPEN / PARTIALLY UNZIPPED
LOCKS:        LOCKED / UNLOCKED / BROKEN
WINDOWS:      CLOSED / OPEN / CRACKED
LETTERS:      FOLDED / UNFOLDED / IN ENVELOPE
SCROLLS:      ROLLED / UNROLLED / PARTIALLY OPEN



CORRECT PROMPT FORMAT:
❌ WRONG (State not specified):
"Ayaan looks down at the wrapped leather book in his hands"
(AI assumes: looking at = reading = book OPEN)

✅ CORRECT (State explicitly specified):
"Ayaan holds the ancient leather book in his gloved hands.
The book is CLOSED - he is holding it protectively against
his chest, NOT reading it. The book cover (as shown in 2nd
reference) faces outward, brass clasp visible. He glances
toward his friend off-frame while securing the CLOSED book."



ACTION vs STATE Conflicts:
Some actions IMPLY a state. Be careful!
ACTION                  → IMPLIED STATE    → SPECIFY INSTEAD
─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
"looks at book"         → OPEN             → "holds CLOSED book, glances at
cover"
"reads the page"        → OPEN             → (only use if book should be
open)
"examines the book"     → OPEN             → "examines the CLOSED book's
cover"
"flips through"         → OPEN             → (only use if book should be
open)
"clutches the book"     → CLOSED           → good! implies closed
"holds against chest"   → CLOSED           → good! implies closed
"opens the book"        → TRANSITION       → describe what's inside when
opened
"looks down at book"    → OPEN             → "looks down at CLOSED book in
hands"
🚨 ACTION-STATE CONSISTENCY (CRITICAL!)
THE PROBLEM: Even when you say “book is closed” in one part of prompt, if your ACTION
implies OPEN, AI will open it!
❌ CONFLICTING PROMPT:
"He holds the wrapped leather-bound book" ← implies CLOSED
...
"looks down at the book" ← implies READING = OPEN!
...
"Book remains identical (dark leather + brass clasp)"

RESULT: AI opened the book because "looks down at" = reading!

SOLUTION: Action must MATCH desired state!
✅ CONSISTENT PROMPT:
"He holds the CLOSED leather-bound book against his chest.
The book is SHUT - cover facing outward, brass clasp visible.
He glances DOWN at the closed cover briefly, then looks up
toward Hamza off-camera.

⚠️ OBJECT STATE LOCK:
- Book: CLOSED throughout - absolutely NO opening
- NO pages visible at any point
- Cover and clasp visible, NOT interior pages
- Character is HOLDING book, NOT reading it"



DANGEROUS PHRASES (Avoid if object should stay CLOSED):
❌ DO NOT USE if book should be CLOSED:
- "looks at the book" (implies reading)
- "looks down at the book" (implies reading)
- "examines the book" (implies opening)
- "studies the book" (implies reading)
- "checks the book" (implies opening)

✅ USE INSTEAD for CLOSED book:
- "holds the closed book against chest"
- "grips the closed book tightly"
- "clutches the book protectively"
- "glances at the book's cover"
- "secures the closed book in his hands"
- "presses the book to his chest"
OBJECT STATE LOCK - ENHANCED FORMAT:
⚠️ OBJECT STATE LOCK (CRITICAL - READ CAREFULLY):

BOOK STATE: CLOSED - MUST REMAIN SHUT!
├── The book is CLOSED throughout entire clip
├── NO pages visible - book is NOT open
├── Cover facing [direction] - brass clasp visible
├── Character is HOLDING it, NOT reading it
├── DO NOT open the book under any circumstances
└── If you show open book = WRONG, regenerate!

ACTIONS COMPATIBLE WITH CLOSED BOOK:
├── Holding against chest ✓
├── Gripping tightly ✓
├── Glancing at cover ✓
├── Clutching protectively ✓
└── Pressing to body ✓

ACTIONS THAT WOULD OPEN BOOK (DO NOT DO):
├── Looking at pages ✗
├── Reading ✗
├── Examining contents ✗
├── Flipping through ✗
└── Studying text ✗



REWRITTEN EXAMPLE - Snow Scene:
❌ ORIGINAL (Caused book to open):
"Action: Ayaan takes a tight breath, looks down at the book,
then lifts his eyes toward Hamza (off-camera)..."

✅ CORRECTED (Keeps book closed):
"Action: Ayaan takes a tight breath, clutching the CLOSED book
against his chest. He glances briefly at the snow-covered
leather cover in his hands, then lifts his eyes toward Hamza
(off-camera) with controlled, fearful determination.

⚠️ OBJECT STATE LOCK:
- Book is CLOSED and remains CLOSED
- Cover facing outward, brass clasp visible
- Snow flakes on the closed cover surface
- Ayaan is HOLDING the book, NOT reading it
- NO pages visible - book stays SHUT
- DO NOT show book open at any point"
VIDEO PROMPT CHECKLIST - Objects:
Before finalizing any prompt with objects, verify:
☐ Is object STATE explicitly specified? (CLOSED/OPEN)
☐ Does ACTION match the desired STATE?
  - "looks at book" = will OPEN it!
  - "holds closed book" = stays CLOSED
☐ Is there an OBJECT STATE LOCK section?
☐ Are DANGEROUS PHRASES avoided?
☐ Is there a "DO NOT" statement for wrong state?



🚨 BREATH/FOG/SMOKE EFFECTS (CRITICAL!)
THE PROBLEM: When you mention “visible breath” or “breath in cold air,” AI creates
DRAMATIC DRAGON-LIKE SMOKE that looks completely unnatural!
❌ WHAT AI GENERATES:
- Thick white smoke pouring from mouth
- Fog going in weird directions (toward objects)
- Constant smoke stream like cigarette
- Looks like character is breathing fire (white)
- Unnatural, distracting, breaks immersion!

REAL COLD BREATH looks like:
✅ NATURAL BREATH:
- Very SUBTLE, thin wisp
- Appears briefly on EXHALE only
- Dissipates within 1 second
- Goes slightly downward/forward
- NOT visible on every breath
- Small puff, not smoke stream



SOLUTION: Avoid or Strictly Control Breath Effects
OPTION 1: AVOID COMPLETELY (Recommended)
Simply don't mention breath at all!
Most viewers won't notice missing breath vapor.
They WILL notice weird dragon smoke.

❌ Remove: "His breath is visible in the cold"
❌ Remove: "Breath visibly puffs"
✅ Instead: Don't mention breath - focus on other cold indicators
OPTION 2: MINIMAL MENTION (Risky)
If you MUST show breath, be VERY specific:

"A TINY, barely-visible wisp of breath vapor appears
briefly on one exhale - very subtle, thin, and
dissipates immediately. NOT thick smoke, NOT dramatic,
NOT constant. Natural and barely noticeable."

OPTION 3: SHOW COLD WITHOUT BREATH
Better ways to show cold environment:
- Snow on clothing/hair
- Red nose/cheeks
- Character shivering slightly
- Frost on eyelashes/beard
- Hunched posture against cold
- Hands rubbing together
- Snow flakes in air
(None of these create weird effects!)



BREATH EFFECT LOCK:
If scene is in cold environment, add this:
⚠️ BREATH/FOG LOCK:
├── NO visible breath vapor (or MINIMAL only)
├── NO thick smoke/fog from mouth
├── NO dragon-like breath effects
├── NO constant steam from breathing
├── If any breath shown: tiny wisp, immediately dissipates
└── Show cold through: snow on clothes, red cheeks, shivering

DO NOT create dramatic breath fog - it looks unnatural!



DANGEROUS PHRASES (Avoid for cold scenes):
❌ AVOID THESE:
- "breath visible in cold"
- "breath puffs out"
- "breath fog"
- "breathing visible"
- "steam from mouth"
- "exhale visible"

These trigger dramatic smoke effects!
✅ USE INSTEAD (to show cold):
- "snow flakes catching on his hood"
- "frost visible on his stubble"
- "cheeks reddened from cold"
- "shoulders hunched against wind"
- "snow accumulating on jacket"



OTHER EFFECTS TO CONTROL:
Similar problems happen with:
FIRE/FLAMES:
❌ "warm glow from fire" → Creates magical orange light everywhere
✅ "small campfire in corner, contained light"

DUST:
❌ "dusty room" → Creates thick haze blocking visibility
✅ "some dust particles visible in sunbeam only"

RAIN:
❌ "heavy rain" → Creates waterfall effect
✅ "light rain, droplets visible on window"

SMOKE:
❌ "smoke in air" → Creates fog machine effect
✅ "thin wisp of smoke rising from [source]"

FOG:
❌ "foggy atmosphere" → Zero visibility
✅ "slight mist in distance, foreground clear"



ATMOSPHERIC EFFECTS CONTROL:
Add this section when scene has weather/atmosphere:
⚠️ ATMOSPHERIC EFFECTS LOCK:
├── Snow: Wind-driven streaks only, clean air between
├── Breath: NONE or barely-visible tiny wisp
├── Fog/Haze: NONE (clean air)
├── Dust: NONE or only in specific light beam
└── Keep atmosphere CLEAN and CLEAR

DO NOT add dramatic atmospheric effects!
Scene should have CLEAR visibility.
COMPLETE OBJECT DESCRIPTION:
When an object appears in a scene, describe:
OBJECT DESCRIPTION CHECKLIST:
├── NAME: What is it? (the ancient leather book)
├── STATE: Open/Closed/Wrapped/etc.? (CLOSED)
├── POSITION: Where is it? (in his gloved hands)
├── ORIENTATION: Which side visible? (cover facing outward)
├── DETAILS: Key features visible? (brass clasp, worn leather)
├── INTERACTION: How is character engaging? (holding protectively)
└── MATCH REFERENCE: (exactly as shown in 2nd attached image)



EXAMPLE - Book in Snow Scene:
❌ WRONG:
"Ayaan looks down at the wrapped leather book in his hands,
then glances toward his friend."

(Problem: "looks down at" implies reading, AI will open the book!)

✅ CORRECT:
"Ayaan holds the ancient leather book (from 2nd reference) in
his gloved hands.

BOOK STATE: CLOSED - the book remains shut throughout.
- Cover facing camera (brass clasp and crescent moon symbol visible)
- Ayaan holds it protectively at chest level
- He is NOT reading it - just securing it from the snow
- Snow flakes landing on the closed cover

He glances to the side toward his friend off-frame,
breath visible in cold air, while keeping the CLOSED
book safe in his grip."



OBJECT STATE LOCK (Add to prompts):
⚠️ OBJECT STATE LOCK:
├── Book: CLOSED throughout - NO opening
├── Cover visible, brass clasp visible
├── Character holds it, does NOT read it
└── State must match reference image (closed)
TEMPLATE UPDATE - Character + Object:
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
📸 IMAGE GENERATION - Clip [X.X]

🚨 CHARACTER IDENTIFICATION FOR GROK (CRITICAL!)
THE PROBLEM: AI (Gemini/Claude) knows character names, but GROK DOESN’T!
❌ WHAT HAPPENS:

Prompt: "Hamza slips. Ayaan grabs Hamza's forearm."

Grok thinks: "Who is Ayaan? Who is Hamza? I just see a
man in a hooded jacket. I'll guess randomly!"

Result: Roles get SWAPPED or confused!

GROK ONLY UNDERSTANDS:
  •   What it can SEE in attached image
  •   Visual descriptions (clothing, position, features)
  •   “The man in the hooded jacket” ✓
  •   “Ayaan” ✗ (meaningless to Grok)


SOLUTION: Use VISUAL IDENTIFIERS, Not Just Names!
Every time you mention a character, describe them visually:
❌ WRONG (Grok doesn't understand):
"Ayaan grabs Hamza's forearm"

✅ CORRECT (Grok understands):
"The man in the dark hooded jacket (visible in the attached
image, holding the book) reaches out and grabs the arm of
his friend (entering from the right side of frame, wearing
a lighter jacket with no hood)."



CHARACTER IDENTIFICATION FORMAT:
When a character appears or acts, identify them by:
CHARACTER VISUAL ID:
├── VISIBLE FEATURES: What can Grok SEE?
│   - Clothing (jacket color, hood up/down)
│   - Position (left/right, foreground/background)
│   - What they're holding (book, torch, etc.)
│   - Distinctive features (beard, no beard, etc.)
│
├── REFERENCE TO IMAGE: Where in attached image?
│   - "The man visible in the attached image"
│   - "The figure on the left side"
│   - "The character holding the book"
│
└── THEN name (for our reference only):
    - "(this is Ayaan)" - for AI/user tracking
TEMPLATE - Single Character in Frame:
"The man visible in the attached image - dark hooded winter
jacket, holding the wrapped leather book against his chest
(this is AYAAN) - [performs action]."

TEMPLATE - Second Character Entering:
"A second figure (this is HAMZA) enters from the right side
of frame - he has a lighter colored jacket, hood down,
visible beard. He [performs action]. The first man (AYAAN -
dark jacket, holding book) reacts by [action]."

TEMPLATE - Two Characters Already in Frame:
"In the attached image, two men are visible:
- LEFT SIDE: Man in dark hooded jacket, holding book (AYAAN)
- RIGHT SIDE: Man in brown bomber jacket, trimmed beard (HAMZA)

The man on the LEFT (dark jacket/AYAAN) speaks while the man
on the RIGHT (brown jacket/HAMZA) reacts."



REWRITING THE EXAMPLE PROMPT:
❌ ORIGINAL (Grok confused):
"Action: Hamza (just off-frame) slips. Ayaan jerks his body
sideways and grabs Hamza's forearm to stop the fall."

✅ CORRECTED (Grok understands):
"Action: The man visible in the attached image - dark hooded
jacket, holding the wrapped book (this is AYAAN) - suddenly
jerks his body sideways. His friend (HAMZA - entering frame
from the RIGHT side, lighter jacket, hood down) has slipped
on the ice. AYAAN (the man with the book) reaches out with
his right arm and grabs the forearm of HAMZA (the man
entering from right).

Visual clarity:
- Man HOLDING BOOK (AYAAN) = stays center, reaches out
- Man ENTERING FROM RIGHT (HAMZA) = the one being grabbed
- Book remains with the hooded figure throughout"



CHARACTER ACTION CLARITY:
For every action, specify WHO does WHAT:
✅ CLEAR FORMAT:
"[VISUAL ID + NAME] performs [ACTION]"

Examples:
- "The hooded man with the book (AYAAN) turns his head"
- "The second man entering from right (HAMZA) stumbles"
- "The figure holding the torch (HAMZA) raises it higher"
- "The man in foreground (AYAAN) reaches toward the other"



GROK IDENTIFICATION CHECKLIST:
Before finalizing any prompt with characters:
☐ Have I described what each character LOOKS LIKE?
  - Clothing (color, type, hood up/down)
  - Position (left/right, center, entering from where)
  - What they're holding

☐ Have I linked visual description to name?
  - "the man in dark hood (AYAAN)"
  - "the figure in brown jacket (HAMZA)"

☐ For actions, have I used VISUAL ID + name?
  - NOT: "Ayaan grabs Hamza"
  - YES: "The hooded man (AYAAN) grabs the other man (HAMZA)"

☐ If character enters frame, have I specified FROM WHERE?
  - "entering from LEFT/RIGHT side of frame"

☐ Is it 100% clear who does what based on VISUAL description alone?



🚨 CHARACTER POSTURE SPECIFICATION (CRITICAL!)
THE PROBLEM: If you don’t specify posture, Grok will CHANGE it randomly!
❌ WHAT HAPPENS:

Last frame: Characters are STANDING on mountain ridge
Prompt: "Ayaan holds the book and looks at Hamza"
(No posture mentioned!)

Grok thinks: "Holding book, looking... maybe reading?
People sit when reading... I'll make them SIT!"

Result: Characters suddenly SITTING in the snow! 🪑❄️
SOLUTION: Always Specify POSTURE!
Posture types to specify:
STANDING POSTURES:
├── Standing upright
├── Standing, leaning against [object]
├── Standing, hunched against wind/cold
├── Standing at edge of [location]
└── Standing, weight on one leg

SITTING POSTURES:
├── Sitting on [chair/rock/ground]
├── Sitting cross-legged
├── Sitting with legs extended
├── Sitting, hunched over
└── Sitting on edge of [object]

CROUCHING/KNEELING:
├── Crouching down
├── Kneeling on one knee
├── Kneeling on both knees
├── Squatting
└── Bent over examining [object]

LYING/RECLINING:
├── Lying on back
├── Lying face down
├── Lying on side
├── Propped up on elbow
└── Collapsed on ground

MOVEMENT POSTURES:
├── Walking forward
├── Walking toward [direction]
├── Running
├── Climbing
├── Stumbling/falling
└── Reaching toward [object]



POSTURE LOCK - Add to every prompt:
⚠️ CHARACTER POSTURE LOCK:

AYAAN (hooded man with book):
├── Posture: STANDING - remains standing throughout
├── NOT sitting, NOT crouching, NOT kneeling
├── Feet planted on snowy ridge
├── Body slightly hunched against wind
└── DO NOT change posture - stays STANDING

HAMZA (man in lighter jacket):
├── Posture: STANDING - remains standing throughout
├── NOT sitting, NOT lying down
├── Standing next to Ayaan
└── DO NOT change posture - stays STANDING



POSTURE MUST MATCH LAST FRAME:
⚠️ POSTURE CONTINUITY:

In attached image (last frame), characters are:
├── AYAAN: STANDING on ridge, hunched against wind
├── HAMZA: STANDING beside him
└── Both characters REMAIN STANDING in this clip

DO NOT change their posture from standing to sitting/kneeling!
Posture must be CONTINUOUS with attached reference.



COMMON POSTURE MISTAKES:

Prompt Issue        What Grok Does            Fix
No posture          Random                    “STANDING throughout”
mentioned           sitting/standing
“looks at book”     Assumes sitting to read   “STANDING, looks at book in hands”
“rests”             Makes them sit/lie        “STANDING, pauses to rest”
                    down
“examines object”   Crouches or sits          “STANDING, examines object at eye
                                              level”
“waits”             Might sit down            “STANDING, waits alertly”


DANGEROUS PHRASES (Imply posture change):
❌ PHRASES THAT MIGHT CHANGE POSTURE:
- "rests for a moment" → might sit down
- "takes a break" → might sit
- "relaxes" → might sit or lie
- "examines closely" → might crouch
- "looks down at" → might bend or crouch
- "settles in" → will definitely sit

✅ SAFE ALTERNATIVES:
- "pauses while standing"
- "stands alert, taking a breath"
- "remains standing, shoulders relaxed slightly"
- "standing, holds object at eye level to examine"
- "standing upright, glances down at object in hands"



COMPLETE CHARACTER DESCRIPTION FORMAT:
When describing a character, include ALL of these:
CHARACTER FULL DESCRIPTION:
├── IDENTITY: Visual description + name
│   "The hooded man in dark jacket (AYAAN)"
│
├── POSTURE: Standing/Sitting/etc.
│   "STANDING on the snowy ridge"
│
├── BODY POSITION: How are they oriented?
│   "Facing toward camera, body angled left"
│
├── WHAT THEY'RE HOLDING: Objects in hands
│   "Holding the wrapped book against his chest"
│
├── EXPRESSION: Face/emotion
│   "Expression shows determination mixed with fear"
│
└── POSTURE LOCK: Stays same throughout
    "Remains STANDING throughout - NO sitting/crouching"



EXAMPLE - Corrected Prompt:
❌ ORIGINAL (Posture not specified):
"The hooded man (AYAAN) holds the book and looks toward
his friend (HAMZA). Hamza reaches out."

✅ CORRECTED (Posture locked):
"The hooded man (AYAAN) is STANDING on the snowy ridge,
feet planted, body hunched slightly against the wind. He
holds the book against his chest. He turns his head to
look toward his friend.

HAMZA is STANDING beside him on the ridge. He reaches out
his arm toward AYAAN.
⚠️ POSTURE LOCK:
- Both characters REMAIN STANDING throughout
- NO sitting down, NO crouching, NO kneeling
- Posture matches attached reference (both standing)
- Feet stay planted on snowy ground"



POSTURE VERIFICATION CHECKLIST:
☐ Have I specified each character's POSTURE?
  - Standing / Sitting / Crouching / etc.

☐ Does posture MATCH the attached reference?
  - If standing in reference → stays standing

☐ Have I added POSTURE LOCK?
  - "Remains STANDING throughout"
  - "DO NOT change posture"

☐ Have I avoided DANGEROUS PHRASES?
  - "rests" / "relaxes" / "settles" → might sit

☐ Is posture CONSISTENT from clip start to end?



🚨 SHOT DURATION & PACING (CRITICAL!)
GROK LIMITATION: Grok generates minimum 6 seconds - this CANNOT be changed
through prompts.
WHY WE CAN’T USE “ACTION COMPLETE EARLY” HACK:
❌ HACK BREAKS LASTFRAME WORKFLOW:

If action completes in 2 seconds → Character freezes
Remaining 4 seconds → Frozen/dead frames
Last frame → Awkward frozen pose
Next clip → Starts from weird frame = DISASTER!

Our workflow depends on LASTFRAME for continuity.
Frozen awkward lastframe = broken chain!



ACTUAL SOLUTION: Plan shots that FILL 6 seconds meaningfully!
Since every clip WILL be 6 seconds, plan content that FILLS 6 seconds:
✅ CORRECT APPROACH:

Instead of: "Feet close-up" (2 sec content stretched to 6)
Plan: "Feet walking through snow, 3-4 steps, crunching" (fills 6 sec)

Instead of: "Character looks at other" (2 sec of just staring)
Plan: "Character notices, processes, expression changes, then looks" (fills 6
sec)

Instead of: "Quick reaction shot" (2 sec reaction)
Plan: "Reaction builds - subtle first, grows, emotion develops" (fills 6 sec)



FILLING 6 SECONDS WITH RICH CONTENT:

                 ❌ Thin Content
Shot Type        (Boring)                 ✅ Rich Content (Engaging)
Feet shot        Just feet standing/one   Feet walking 3-4 steps, snow kicking, texture
                 step                     detail
Reaction         Just staring             Notice → Process → React → Emotion deepens →
                                          Hold
Look at          Quick glance held        Sense something → Turn slowly → Find person →
person           forever                  Expression shifts
Detail shot      Object sitting static    Camera slowly reveals, light plays, dust particles
Establishing     Static wide              Slow push in + movement in scene (birds, wind,
                                          people)
Dialogue         Just talking head        Gestures, micro-expressions, breathing, natural
                                          movement


PACING THROUGH CONTENT DENSITY:
Since duration is FIXED at 6 seconds, control pacing through:
FAST PACING (Action scenes):
├── MORE shots covering same story time
├── MORE action WITHIN each 6-sec clip
├── MORE camera movement
├── MORE things happening simultaneously
├── DENSER content per clip
└── Post-edit: Trim clips shorter if needed

SLOW PACING (Emotional scenes):
├── FEWER shots
├── LESS action, more atmosphere
├── SLOWER camera movement
├── Single focused action per clip
├── Breathing room
└── Post-edit: Keep full 6 sec



RICH CONTENT EXAMPLES:
Feet Shot - BEFORE vs AFTER:
❌ THIN (Boring 6 seconds):
"Close-up of Ayaan's boots in snow."
(What happens for 6 seconds? Just... boots?)

✅ RICH (Engaging 6 seconds):
"Close-up of Ayaan's boots WALKING through deep snow.
Three deliberate steps - each step crunches into fresh powder,
snow compresses under weight, small spray of white kicks up.
Camera follows the walking feet, tracking alongside.
Fourth step plants firmly as he pauses."
(Full action for 6 seconds!)

Reaction Shot - BEFORE vs AFTER:
❌ THIN (Boring 6 seconds):
"Hamza looks worried."
(Just... staring worried for 6 seconds?)

✅ RICH (Engaging 6 seconds):
"Hamza's expression shifts - first he's focused ahead,
then something catches his attention (ears perk).
He turns his head 30 degrees right, eyes searching.
Finds what concerned him. Brow furrows gradually.
Jaw tightens. Worry settles into his features.
He swallows once, steeling himself."
(Story within the reaction!)

Establishing Shot - BEFORE vs AFTER:
❌ THIN (Boring 6 seconds):
"Wide shot of the haveli."
(Static building for 6 seconds = boring!)

✅ RICH (Engaging 6 seconds):
"Wide shot of the haveli in golden morning light.
Camera slowly pushes forward toward the building.
A flock of pigeons takes flight from the roof.
Wind rustles the trees on either side.
Dust particles catch the sunlight.
The ancient building slowly grows larger in frame."
(Movement + atmosphere fills 6 seconds!)



POST-PRODUCTION TRIMMING:
For final varied pacing, trim in editing software:
WORKFLOW:
1. Generate all clips at 6 seconds (required)
2. Content fills all 6 seconds naturally
3. In video editor (CapCut, Premiere, etc.):
   - Keep clips that need full duration
   - Trim clips where story needs faster pace
   - Cut on action, not on dead frames
   - Lastframe remains good because content continued!

CONTENT DENSITY GUIDE:
FILL every 6-second clip with:

FOR STATIC SUBJECTS (objects, buildings):
├── Camera movement (slow push, pan, crane)
├── Environmental movement (wind, birds, dust)
├── Light play (clouds, shadows moving)
└── Atmospheric elements (appropriate to scene)

FOR CHARACTER SHOTS:
├── Continuous subtle movement (breathing, shifting)
├── Expression evolution (not static face)
├── Physical business (touching, adjusting)
├── Motivated action (reason to move)
└── Environmental interaction

FOR ACTION SHOTS:
├── Extended action sequence (multiple beats)
├── Build-up → action → follow-through
├── Environmental response to action
└── Continuous movement throughout



🚨 CAMERA REVEAL RULE (CRITICAL!)
THE PROBLEM: Camera movement that “reveals” new elements NOT in the reference
image causes those elements to MAGICALLY APPEAR!
❌ EXAMPLE OF THE PROBLEM:

Attached image: Lane with chai stall (shops NOT visible on right)
Prompt: "Camera pans right to reveal shuttered shops"

What happens:
├── Frame 1: Lane and chai stall (matching reference)
├── Frame 2: Camera pans right...
├── Frame 3: Shops SUDDENLY APPEAR! 👻
└── Frame 4: Full shops visible (spawned from nothing!)

Looks MAGICAL and UNNATURAL - shops materialized!



THE RULE:
🔴 CAMERA CANNOT "REVEAL" WHAT ISN'T IN THE REFERENCE!

If element is NOT visible in attached image →
Camera movement CANNOT bring it "into view" →
Because Grok will SPAWN it from nowhere!

Camera can only:
├── Move TOWARD elements already visible
├── Get CLOSER to elements already in frame
├── Move WITHIN the visible scene area
└── NOT pan/tilt to areas not shown in reference
SOLUTION: Two Approaches
APPROACH 1: Generate NEW IMAGE First (Recommended)
If you need to show new elements (shops, buildings, etc.):

STEP 1: Generate NEW IMAGE that includes those elements
        "Wide shot of the lane, chai stall on LEFT,
        row of shuttered shops visible on RIGHT,
        including the old bookshop with faded signboard"

STEP 2: Use this NEW IMAGE as video reference

STEP 3: Now camera can move toward the shops
        (because they're already IN the reference!)

The elements exist in the reference →
Camera moves toward them →
Natural movement, no spawning!

APPROACH 2: Limit Camera to Visible Area
If using existing image that doesn't show shops:

ONLY move camera within what's already visible:
├── Dolly forward down the lane ✓ (lane is visible)
├── Push toward chai stall ✓ (chai stall is visible)
├── Pan right to reveal shops ✗ (shops NOT in image!)

Adjust the shot to work with what's IN the reference.



CHECKING CAMERA MOVEMENTS:
Before writing any camera movement, ask:
☐ Where is camera moving TO?
☐ Is that destination VISIBLE in the reference image?
  - YES → Movement is valid ✓
  - NO → Movement will cause SPAWNING! ✗
☐ What NEW things come into frame during movement?
  - If anything NEW → It will be SPAWNED → Problem!



VALID vs INVALID Camera Movements:
Reference image shows: Lane center, chai stall left, buildings ahead

✅ VALID MOVEMENTS (toward visible elements):
├── Dolly forward down lane (lane is visible)
├── Push toward chai stall (chai stall is visible)
├── Crane up showing rooftops (buildings ahead visible)
├── Track left toward chai stall (visible)
└── Slight pan within visible area (OK)

❌ INVALID MOVEMENTS (toward non-visible elements):
├── Pan right to reveal shops (shops NOT in image!)
├── Pan left to show another street (NOT in image!)
├── Turn around to show what's behind (NOT in image!)
├── Tilt down to show ground detail (if NOT visible!)
└── Any movement revealing NEW elements



PROMPT STRUCTURE FOR NEW ELEMENTS:
When scene needs to show new elements:
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
📸 FIRST: Generate NEW IMAGE with all elements visible
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

 ├── LEFT side: Chai stall with steam rising

 ├── BACKGROUND: More buildings, morning sky

 16:9, photorealistic, crisp morning sunlight."

because shops are ALREADY VISIBLE in the reference!

CAMERA REVEAL CHECKLIST:
Before any camera movement that "reveals" something:

☐ Is the element VISIBLE in attached reference image?
  - YES → OK to reveal/move toward it
  - NO → STOP! Generate new image first!

☐ Will camera movement show NEW areas not in reference?
  - YES → Those areas will be SPAWNED → Problem!
  - NO → Safe to proceed
☐ Does the prompt say "reveal" or "bring into view"?

  - Generate NEW IMAGE that includes element first

  - YES → Movement is safe ✓
  - NO → Need new reference image ✗



ELEMENT INTRODUCTION WORKFLOW:
When introducing NEW elements (shops, buildings, characters):

1⃣ CHECK: Is element in current reference?
    │
    ├── YES → Proceed with video prompt
    │
    └── NO → Go to step 2

2⃣ GENERATE: New image WITH element included
    "Create image showing [current scene] PLUS [new element]"

3⃣ USE: New image as reference for video prompt

4⃣ NOW: Camera can move toward the element naturally
    (Because it was always there in the reference!)
