Instead of just names, always pair with visual:

Name Only ❌         Visual + Name ✅
“Ayaan speaks”      “The hooded man (AYAAN) speaks”
“Hamza enters”      “A second figure in brown jacket (HAMZA) enters from right”
“Zain holds book”   “The man holding the book (ZAIN) stands”
“Naeem appears”     “A figure in dark coat (NAEEM) appears in doorway”


🚨 CLIP-TO-CLIP CONTINUITY (CRITICAL!)
When creating continuous clips from lastframes, THREE major issues occur:
PROBLEM 1: Camera Speed Changes PROBLEM 2: Color Drift
PROBLEM 3: Expression Shifts


PROBLEM 1: CAMERA SPEED INCONSISTENCY
THE ISSUE:
Clip 1: Dolly in at SLOW speed
Clip 2: Dolly in continues but FASTER
Clip 3: Dolly in continues but SLOWER

Grok starts each clip fresh - doesn't remember previous speed!
Result: Jerky, inconsistent movement across cuts.

SOLUTION: Explicit Speed Specification
Define speed with MEASURABLE terms:
SPEED DEFINITIONS:
├── VERY SLOW: Almost imperceptible movement (1-2 inches per second)
├── SLOW: Gentle, deliberate movement (3-5 inches per second)
├── MEDIUM: Normal walking pace movement
├── FAST: Quick, urgent movement
└── VERY FAST: Rapid, action-sequence movement

Camera Speed Lock Template:
⚠️ CAMERA SPEED LOCK:

Movement Type: DOLLY IN (forward toward subject)
Speed: VERY SLOW - approximately 2-3 inches per second
Consistency: This EXACT speed must match previous clip
Reference: Camera was moving at this speed in attached image

SPECIFIC MEASUREMENT:
- In 6 seconds, camera moves forward approximately 12-18 inches
- Movement is BARELY PERCEPTIBLE but continuous
- DO NOT speed up or slow down from previous clip
- Maintain IDENTICAL pace throughout

Speed comparison:
- NOT as slow as completely static
- NOT as fast as walking pace
- EXACTLY matching the gentle drift of previous clip

Speed Description Phrases:

Speed Level   Use These Phrases
Very Slow     “barely perceptible drift,” “glacial pace,” “almost static”
Slow          “gentle movement,” “deliberate drift,” “leisurely pace”
Medium        “steady movement,” “walking pace,” “moderate speed”
Fast          “quick movement,” “urgent pace,” “rapid motion”

Example - Continuous Dolly:
CLIP 2.1:
"Camera begins VERY SLOW dolly in toward the desk.
Speed: Glacial pace, barely perceptible forward drift.
In 6 seconds, camera moves approximately 12-15 inches closer."

CLIP 2.2:
"⚠️ CAMERA SPEED CONTINUITY:
Continue the EXACT same dolly in motion from previous clip.
Speed: VERY SLOW - IDENTICAL to previous clip's pace.
The movement should feel like ONE continuous shot.
In 6 seconds, camera continues forward another 12-15 inches.
DO NOT change speed - maintain the glacial drift."

CLIP 2.3:
"⚠️ CAMERA SPEED CONTINUITY:
SAME dolly in, SAME speed as previous two clips.
Speed: VERY SLOW - matching exactly.
This should feel like minute 0:12-0:18 of one long take.
NO acceleration, NO deceleration."



PROBLEM 2: COLOR DRIFT
THE ISSUE:
Clip 1: Steel-blue, medium brightness
Clip 2: Slightly LIGHTER, more cyan
Clip 3: DARKER, more saturated
Clip 4: Color has drifted significantly!

Small changes accumulate into big differences!

SOLUTION: Precise Color Anchoring
Use SPECIFIC color values, not just descriptions:
⚠️ COLOR PRECISION LOCK:

Instead of: "cold steel-blue tone"
Use: "Color temperature approximately 6500K (cold daylight)
     Blue channel: Dominant
     Shadow tone: Deep navy (#1a2634)
     Midtone: Steel blue-grey (#4a6580)
     Highlight: Cool white with slight blue (#e0e8f0)"

EXPOSURE LOCK:
- Brightness: 40% (slightly underexposed for mood)
- Contrast: 65% (high contrast for drama)
- DO NOT brighten or darken from reference

SPECIFIC INSTRUCTIONS:
- Match the EXACT color values of attached reference
- NO warming shift (no orange/yellow creeping in)
- NO increased saturation
- NO lifted shadows (keep them deep)
- NO crushed blacks (maintain shadow detail)

Color Drift Prevention Template:
⚠️ COLOR MATCHING - ZERO TOLERANCE:

Reference image color profile:
├── Temperature: COLD (blue-dominant, ~6500K)
├── Tint: Neutral to slightly cyan
├── Shadows: Deep navy, NOT pure black
├── Midtones: Steel grey-blue
├── Highlights: Cool white, NOT warm
├── Saturation: Medium (NOT oversaturated)
└── Contrast: High but not crushed

MATCH EXACTLY - NO DRIFT ALLOWED:
├── DO NOT shift warmer (no yellow/orange)
├── DO NOT shift cooler (no excessive blue)
├── DO NOT increase saturation
├── DO NOT decrease saturation
├── DO NOT lighten shadows
├── DO NOT darken highlights
├── DO NOT add any color cast not in reference
└── First frame MUST match last frame of previous clip EXACTLY

If in doubt, ERR ON THE SIDE OF MATCHING REFERENCE.

Scene Color Passport:
Create a “color passport” at scene start that ALL clips reference:
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
🎨 SCENE COLOR PASSPORT - Scene 5 (Snowy Ridge)
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

MASTER COLOR VALUES (All clips must match):
├── Overall Tone: Cold steel-blue night
├── Color Temp: ~6500K (cold)
├── Primary Color: Navy blue (#1e3a5f)
├── Secondary: Steel grey (#708090)
├── Accent: Snow white with blue tint (#e8f0f8)
├── Shadow Density: 70% (deep but not crushed)
├── Highlight: Cool white, no warmth
├── Saturation: 45% (slightly desaturated)
├── Contrast: 70% (high)
└── Overall Exposure: -0.5 stops (moody/dark)

ALL CLIPS IN SCENE 5 MUST MATCH THESE VALUES.
Reference this passport in every prompt.
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════



PROBLEM 3: EXPRESSION SHIFTS
THE ISSUE:
Clip 1: Character has DETERMINED expression
Clip 2: Now looks WORRIED
Clip 3: Now looks ANGRY
Clip 4: Now looks NEUTRAL
Face emotions randomly change each clip!

SOLUTION: Expression Lock with Specific Details
Don’t just name emotion - DESCRIBE the physical features:
⚠️ EXPRESSION LOCK:

Instead of: "determined expression"

Use: "EXPRESSION DETAILS (Match reference exactly):
     ├── Eyebrows: Slightly furrowed, pulled together
     ├── Eyes: Focused, looking [direction], slightly narrowed
     ├── Forehead: Light tension lines visible
     ├── Mouth: Closed, lips pressed slightly together
     ├── Jaw: Set firm, slight tension in muscles
     ├── Overall: Determination/resolve
     └── DO NOT CHANGE - expression stays EXACTLY the same

     What this is NOT:
     ├── NOT smiling or relaxed
     ├── NOT angry (no bared teeth, no wide eyes)
     ├── NOT sad (no drooping features)
     ├── NOT scared (no raised eyebrows, no open mouth)
     └── NOT surprised (no wide eyes)"

Expression Description Template:
CHARACTER EXPRESSION LOCK:

The hooded man (AYAAN) expression (MAINTAIN EXACTLY):
├── EYEBROWS: [Raised/Neutral/Furrowed] - [how much]
├── EYES: [Wide/Normal/Narrowed] - looking [direction]
├── FOREHEAD: [Smooth/Light lines/Deep furrow]
├── MOUTH: [Open/Closed/Slightly parted] - [shape]
├── JAW: [Relaxed/Tense/Clenched]
├── OVERALL EMOTION: [Name it]
└── REFERENCE: Expression EXACTLY matches attached image

DO NOT CHANGE EXPRESSION unless scripted!
The face should look like a FREEZE FRAME of attached image
but with subtle natural micro-movements only.

Expression Continuity Across Clips:
CLIP 3.1:
"AYAAN's expression: Determined and alert.
Eyebrows slightly furrowed, eyes focused ahead,
mouth closed and firm, jaw set."
CLIP 3.2:
"⚠️ EXPRESSION CONTINUITY:
AYAAN's expression MUST MATCH previous clip exactly:
- Same furrowed eyebrows
- Same focused eyes
- Same closed, firm mouth
- Same set jaw
DO NOT shift to different emotion.
Face should look like continuous footage of same moment."

CLIP 3.3:
"⚠️ EXPRESSION CONTINUITY:
MAINTAIN IDENTICAL expression from previous clips.
This is the SAME moment, emotions haven't changed.
Any shift in expression = WRONG."



COMPLETE CONTINUITY TEMPLATE:
For clips using lastframe, include ALL THREE locks:
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
⚠️ CLIP-TO-CLIP CONTINUITY LOCKS:
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

1⃣ CAMERA SPEED LOCK:
├── Movement: [Type - dolly/pan/crane/static]
├── Speed: [VERY SLOW/SLOW/MEDIUM]
├── Measurement: Approximately [X] inches in [Y] seconds
├── Consistency: IDENTICAL to previous clip
└── Feel: Should match as ONE continuous take

2⃣ COLOR PRECISION LOCK:
├── Temperature: [Warm/Neutral/Cold] - [K value if possible]
├── Shadows: [Description + hex if possible]
├── Midtones: [Description]
├── Highlights: [Description]
├── Saturation: [Low/Medium/High - percentage]
├── Contrast: [Low/Medium/High - percentage]
├── Match: EXACTLY match attached reference
└── Drift: ZERO tolerance for color shift

3⃣ EXPRESSION LOCK:
├── Character: [Name + visual ID]
├── Eyebrows: [Position and intensity]
├── Eyes: [State + direction]
├── Mouth: [Open/closed + shape]
├── Jaw: [Tension level]
├── Emotion: [Overall feeling]
├── Match: EXACTLY match attached reference
└── Change: NO expression change unless scripted

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════



PRACTICAL EXAMPLE - 3 Continuous Clips:
CLIP 5.1:
"Camera begins VERY SLOW dolly in toward the desk.
Color: Cold steel-blue, shadows deep navy.
Character expression: Determined - furrowed brows, firm mouth.

Speed: Glacial pace, ~15 inches forward in 6 seconds.
This establishes the movement that continues in next clips."

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

CLIP 5.2:
⚠️ CONTINUITY LOCKS:

CAMERA SPEED: Continue EXACT same dolly in.
├── Speed: VERY SLOW - identical to Clip 5.1
├── Movement: ~15 inches forward in 6 seconds
├── Feel: Seamless continuation, ONE long take
└── NO speed change from previous clip

COLOR: Match Clip 5.1 lastframe EXACTLY.
├── Same cold steel-blue temperature
├── Same shadow depth
├── Same contrast level
├── ZERO drift - if anything looks different, it's WRONG
└── First frame of 5.2 = Last frame of 5.1

EXPRESSION: IDENTICAL to Clip 5.1 end.
├── Same furrowed eyebrows
├── Same focused eyes
├── Same firm mouth, set jaw
├── NO emotion shift
└── Face is frozen continuation of previous

────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

CLIP 5.3:
⚠️ CONTINUITY LOCKS:

CAMERA SPEED: Continue EXACT same dolly in (3rd segment).
├── Speed: VERY SLOW - identical to Clips 5.1 and 5.2
├── This is seconds 12-18 of one continuous take
└── NO speed variation

COLOR: Match Clip 5.2 lastframe EXACTLY.
├── Same values as entire scene
├── ZERO accumulated drift
└── Compare to Clip 5.1 - should look identical

EXPRESSION: IDENTICAL to previous clips.
├── No change in facial features
├── Same emotion throughout
└── 18 seconds of same expression



⚠️ CONTINUITY QUALITY CHECK:
After generating clips, verify:
☐ CAMERA TEST: Play clips back-to-back
  - Does movement feel like ONE continuous shot?
  - Any jarring speed changes at cut points?

☐ COLOR TEST: Compare first frames
  - Put Clip 1 last frame next to Clip 2 first frame
  - Any visible color difference? Should be ZERO.

☐ EXPRESSION TEST: Compare faces
  - Freeze on face at each cut point
  - Expression identical? Should be exact match.



Step 3.0E: STORYTELLING IN EVERY PROMPT (CRITICAL!)


🎵 AUDIO SYSTEM - Complete Guide
Audio has FOUR components that must be handled separately:
  1.   NARRATION - Voice-over storytelling
  2.   DIALOGUE - Character speech
  3.   BACKGROUND MUSIC - Mood/atmosphere music
  4.   SOUND EFFECTS - Environmental/action sounds


1️⃣ NARRATION FORMAT
⚠️ NARRATION QUALITY - MOST CRITICAL SECTION! ⚠️
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║                                                               ║
║   GENERIC POETRY ≠ GOOD NARRATION                           ║
║                                                               ║
║   "Kuch raaz safar maangte hain..." = GARBAGE!              ║
║                                                               ║
║   This is the #1 mistake AI makes!                          ║
║   DO NOT write vague philosophical poetry!                    ║
║                                                               ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝



THE NARRATION DISEASE - “POETRY SYNDROME”
❌ SYMPTOMS OF BAD NARRATION (Poetry Syndrome):

1. EVERY line has "..." dramatic pause
2. EVERY sentence sounds philosophical
3. ZERO specific details (names, places, dates)
4. ZERO story information
5. Sounds like shayari, not storytelling
6. Audience learns NOTHING new

EXAMPLE OF THE DISEASE:
"Faisalabad ki in purani galiyon mein...
kuch jagahen aisi hoti hain...
jo sirf ghar nahi hotin... yaad hoti hain.
Aur kuch yaadein... insaan se sirf waqt nahi...
himmat bhi maangti hain."

WHAT'S WRONG:
├── "kuch jagahen" = WHICH places? NAME them!
├── "yaad hoti hain" = WHOSE memory? WHAT happened?
├── "himmat maangti hain" = Generic philosophy!
├── Every line = "..." = Overdramatic!
├── Audience learns = ABSOLUTELY NOTHING!
└── This is LAZY WRITING disguised as depth!



NARRATION QUALITY TEST - 5 QUESTIONS
Before finalizing ANY narration, ask these 5 questions:
╔═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║ QUESTION 1: Does it have SPECIFIC NAMES?                      ║
║ ❌ "ek shakhs" → ✅ "Professor Danish Mirza"                      ║
║ ❌ "purani gali" → ✅ "Aminpur Bazaar ki Haveli #47"              ║
║ ❌ "ek kitaab" → ✅ "1847 ki handwritten diary"                   ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ QUESTION 2: Does it have SPECIFIC NUMBERS?                    ║
║ ❌ "kaafi saal pehle" → ✅ "35 saal pehle, 1989 mein"             ║
║ ❌ "door tak" → ✅ "487 kilometer door, Kashmir"                  ║
║ ❌ "kuch log" → ✅ "sirf 3 log jaante the"                        ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ QUESTION 3: Does it CREATE A QUESTION in audience mind?       ║
║ ❌ "raaz tha" → ✅ "raaz jo 3 logon ki jaan le chuka tha"         ║
║ (Audience thinks: "KAUN mare? KYUN? KYA raaz tha?")           ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ QUESTION 4: Does it have STAKES (consequences)?               ║
║ ❌ "mushkil tha" → ✅ "fail hue to wapis nahi aa sakte"           ║
║ ❌ "khatarnak" → ✅ "pichle 3 jo gaye woh laute nahi"             ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ QUESTION 5: Is it FREE of excessive "..." pauses?             ║
║ ❌ "Kuch raaz... safar maangte... aur kuch..."                  ║
║ ✅ "35 saal pehle Danish Sahab yahan se nikle the.              ║
║     Unke haath mein ek naqsha tha. Woh laute nahi."           ║
╚═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

If ANY answer is NO → REWRITE the narration!



❌ vs ✅ FULL NARRATION COMPARISON
SAME STORY - Two Ways:
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
❌ GARBAGE NARRATION (Poetry Syndrome):
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

"Faisalabad ki in purani galiyon mein...
kuch jagahen aisi hoti hain... jo sirf ghar nahi hotin...
yaad hoti hain.

Aur kuch yaadein... insaan se sirf waqt nahi...
himmat bhi maangti hain.

Is kahani ka raasta... ek kitaab se khulta hai.
Aur ek qasam se.

Do dost... ek hi bike... aur ek aisa cipher...
jo seedha Kashmir tak le jaata hai.

Lekin... jab raaz ki talash shuru hoti hai...
raaz bhi talash shuru kar deta hai."
PROBLEMS:
├── "kuch jagahen" = WHICH? No name!
├── "yaadein himmat maangti" = Generic philosophy!
├── "ek kitaab" = WHICH book? What's in it?
├── "ek qasam" = WHAT promise? To whom?
├── "cipher" = What cipher? Where from?
├── "raaz talash karta hai" = Meaningless poetry!
├── EVERY line has "..." = Overdramatic!
└── Information given = ZERO!

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
✅ ENGAGING NARRATION (Proper Storytelling):
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

"Faisalabad ke Aminpur Bazaar mein ek haveli hai,
number 47. Log ise 'Kitaabon Wala Ghar' kehte hain.

35 saal pehle, 1989 ki ek raat, yahan se Professor
Danish Mirza ghayab ho gaye. Unke haath mein ek
naqsha tha - 1847 ki Dogra War ka - jo seedha
Kashmir ke un pahadon tak le jaata hai jahan
aaj bhi koi nahi jaata.

Danish Sahab ke pote, Zain ne jab dada ki purani
diary kholi, usne dekha ek cipher - Dogra code -
jo 150 saal se koi crack nahi kar paaya tha.

Diary ke aakhri page pe likha tha:
'Jo yeh raaz dhundne jayega... woh akela nahi jayega.
Raaz bhi uske saath chalega.'

Aur Zain ne faisla kar liya - apne dost Hamza ke
saath, ek purani bike pe, 487 kilometer ka safar.
Faisalabad se Kashmir. Jawab dhundne."

WHAT'S BETTER:
├── "Haveli #47, Aminpur Bazaar" = SPECIFIC!
├── "35 saal pehle, 1989" = SPECIFIC time!
├── "Professor Danish Mirza" = SPECIFIC name!
├── "1847 Dogra War naqsha" = SPECIFIC object!
├── "Pote Zain, dost Hamza" = SPECIFIC characters!
├── "487 kilometer" = SPECIFIC distance!
├── "Dogra code, 150 saal" = SPECIFIC mystery!
├── "Diary quote" = SPECIFIC hook!
├── Audience CURIOSITY = HIGH!
└── Story SETUP = COMPLETE!
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
BANNED PHRASES - NEVER USE!
╔═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║ ❌ BANNED - NEVER WRITE THESE:                                   ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ • "Kuch raaz... safar maangte hain"                          ║
║ • "Kuch kahaniyan... sunai nahi jaatin"                      ║
║ • "Waqt ke saath... kuch cheezein badal jaati hain"          ║
║ • "Har jagah ki... apni kahani hoti hai"                     ║
║ • "Kuch yaadein... waqt nahi mitaa sakta"                    ║
║ • "In purani galiyon mein... waqt thehar gaya"               ║
║ • "Zindagi mein... kuch mod aise aate hain"                  ║
║ • "Manzil se zyada... safar zaroori hai"                     ║
║                                                                ║
║ These are LAZY, GENERIC, MEANINGLESS!                          ║
╚═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝



OPENING NARRATION TEMPLATES
Template 1: Location + Event + Mystery
"[Specific Place] mein ek [building] hai.
[X] saal pehle yahan [event] hua tha.
[Name] ke saath [thing] tha - jo aaj tak [mystery]."

Template 2: Character + Discovery + Stakes
"[Name] ko nahi pata tha ki [object] mein [thing] milegi.
Ek aisi [desc] jo [X years] pehle [person] ne chhupai thi.
Aur jo dhundne gaye... [stakes]."



CLIP-WISE NARRATION FLOW


⚠️ CRITICAL: Narration is NOT per clip - JUST LIKE BG MUSIC!
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║                                                              ║
║   NARRATION = ONE continuous audio across MULTIPLE clips    ║
║                                                              ║
║   Same as BG Music:                                          ║
║   • BG Music: 3-6 clips = ONE track                         ║
║   • Narration: 3-5 clips = ONE continuous voiceover         ║
║                                                              ║
║   NOT separate narration for each 6-second clip!            ║
║                                                               ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
❌ WRONG (Per-clip narration - CHOPPY & UNNATURAL!):

Clip 1.1 Narration: "Faisalabad ki galiyon mein..."
Clip 1.2 Narration: "Kuch jagahen yaad hoti hain..."
Clip 1.3 Narration: "Is kahani ka raasta..."
Clip 1.4 Narration: "Do dost, ek bike..."

PROBLEMS:
├── Sounds like SEPARATE voiceovers glued together
├── No natural FLOW
├── Audience notices the CUTS
├── Real movies NEVER do this!
└── This is NOT how professional narration works!



✅ CORRECT (Multi-clip continuous - LIKE BG MUSIC!):

🎙️ NARRATION BLOCK - Clips 1.1 to 1.4

CLIPS COVERED: 1.1, 1.2, 1.3, 1.4
TOTAL DURATION: 24 seconds (4 clips × 6 sec)

ONE CONTINUOUS NARRATION (recorded as single audio):
"Faisalabad ke Aminpur Bazaar mein ek haveli hai,
number 47. 35 saal pehle, 1989 ki ek raat,
Professor Danish Mirza ghayab ho gaye. Unke haath
mein ek naqsha tha - 1847 ki Dogra War ka - jo
seedha Kashmir ke un pahadon tak le jaata hai
jahan aaj bhi koi nahi jaata. Unke pote Zain ne
jab dada ki diary kholi, usne dekha woh cipher
jo 150 saal se koi crack nahi kar paaya."

TIMING BREAKDOWN (for reference only):
├── During Clip 1.1 (0-6 sec): "Faisalabad ke...number 47."
├── During Clip 1.2 (6-12 sec): "35 saal pehle...ghayab ho gaye."
├── During Clip 1.3 (12-18 sec): "Unke haath mein...nahi jaata."
└── During Clip 1.4 (18-24 sec): "Unke pote Zain...kar paaya."

NOTE: This is ONE audio file, not 4 separate recordings!



NARRATION BLOCK STRUCTURE (Like BG Music):
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
🎙️ NARRATION BLOCK - [Scene/Clips Description]
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
CLIPS COVERED: [X.1] - [X.4] (3-5 clips typically)
TOTAL DURATION: [X] seconds

STYLE: [Age], [gender], [tone], [pace]

FULL NARRATION (ONE continuous recording):
"[Complete narration text that flows naturally across
all covered clips. Written and recorded as ONE piece,
not separate chunks. Natural pauses where needed,
but flows as single voiceover.]"

VISUAL SYNC GUIDE (for editing reference):
├── Clip X.1: "[What's on screen]" → "[narration portion]"
├── Clip X.2: "[What's on screen]" → "[narration portion]"
├── Clip X.3: "[What's on screen]" → "[narration portion]"
└── Clip X.4: "[What's on screen]" → "[narration portion]"

════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════



SCENE AUDIO PLANNING (Narration + Music Together):
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
📋 SCENE 1: Opening - COMPLETE AUDIO PLAN
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

TOTAL CLIPS: 1.1 - 1.6 (6 clips = 36 seconds)

🎶 BG MUSIC:
├── Clips 1.1-1.6: ONE track (36 sec)
├── Track: "Mystery Unfolding" - Pixabay
└── Fade in 1.1, fade out 1.6

🎙️ NARRATION BLOCKS:
├── Clips 1.1-1.4: ONE narration (24 sec)
│   "[Full opening narration - introducing story]"
│
├── Clips 1.5-1.6: SILENT (12 sec)
│   (Let visuals breathe, music only)

🗣️ DIALOGUE: None in this scene

🔊 SFX: Per clip as needed

════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
NARRATION COVERAGE GUIDE:
TYPICAL SCENE (6-8 clips = 36-48 seconds):

Anchor Image Method: Opening narration + silence
├── Clips 1-4: Narration block (24 sec)
└── Clips 5-8: Silent/dialogue (24 sec)

Anchor Image Method: Bookend narration
├── Clips 1-3: Opening narration (18 sec)
├── Clips 4-6: Silent/dialogue (18 sec)
└── Clips 7-8: Closing narration (12 sec)

Option C: Mid-scene narration
├── Clips 1-2: Silent establishing (12 sec)
├── Clips 3-5: Narration block (18 sec)
└── Clips 6-8: Silent/dialogue (18 sec)

NEVER: Separate narration for each clip!



SILENCE IS POWERFUL!
60-70% clips: SILENT or dialogue only
30-40% clips: Narration

NOT every scene needs narration!
NOT every clip within narration scenes needs voiceover!

USE NARRATION FOR:
├── Story opening (set up world)
├── Time jumps ("6 mahine baad...")
├── Location changes
├── Backstory/exposition
└── Emotional context

STAY SILENT FOR:
├── Action sequences
├── Dialogue scenes
├── Discovery moments
├── Tension peaks
├── Emotional climaxes
└── Visual beauty shots



NARRATION TEMPLATE
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
🎙️ NARRATION BLOCK - Scene [X]
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
CLIPS COVERED: [X.1] - [X.4]
TOTAL DURATION: [X] seconds

STYLE: [Age], [gender], [tone]

FULL NARRATION (One continuous recording):
"[MUST pass 5 Question Test! Specific names, numbers,
places. Creates curiosity. Has stakes. NO generic poetry!
Flows naturally across all clips as ONE voiceover.]"

✅ QUALITY CHECK (All must be YES):
├── Specific names? [YES/NO]
├── Specific numbers? [YES/NO]
├── Creates curiosity? [YES/NO]
├── Has stakes? [YES/NO]
├── No excessive "..."? [YES/NO]
└── Flows as ONE continuous piece? [YES/NO]

VISUAL SYNC:
├── Clip X.1: [visual] → [narration portion]
├── Clip X.2: [visual] → [narration portion]
├── Clip X.3: [visual] → [narration portion]
└── Clip X.4: [visual] → [narration portion]

════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════




2️⃣ DIALOGUE FORMAT


⚠️ DIALOGUE QUALITY - CRITICAL RULES! ⚠️
╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║                                                               ║
║   GENERIC FILMY LINES ≠ GOOD DIALOGUE                       ║
║                                                               ║
║   "Copy." "Samajh gaya." "Roger that." = GARBAGE!           ║
║                                                               ║
║   Real people DON'T talk like military movies!              ║
║                                                               ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
THE DIALOGUE DISEASE - “FILMY SYNDROME”
❌ SYMPTOMS OF BAD DIALOGUE:

1. Generic military/spy clichés ("Copy", "Roger", "Target acquired")
2. Fake deep philosophy ("Ilm amanat hai", "Waqt sabka aata hai")
3. Information dumps disguised as conversation
4. Every character sounds THE SAME
5. No natural pauses, hesitations, or incomplete sentences
6. Sounds like BAD DUBBING, not real talk

EXAMPLE OF THE DISEASE:
"Plan?"
"Simple nahi. Seedha zaroor."
"Copy."
"Samajh gaya."
"Ready?"
"Ilm amanat hai. Ready rehna parta hai."

WHAT'S WRONG:
├── "Copy" "Samajh gaya" = Military movie clichés!
├── "Ilm amanat hai" = Fake philosophy (cringe!)
├── "Simple nahi. Seedha zaroor" = Trying too hard to sound cool
├── Both characters sound IDENTICAL
├── Real friends NEVER talk like this!
└── This is LAZY WRITING!



DIALOGUE QUALITY TEST - 5 QUESTIONS
Before finalizing ANY dialogue, ask:
╔═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║ Q1: Would a REAL person say this in real life?               ║
║ ❌ "Ilm amanat hai" → ✅ "Yaar, serious hai yeh"                  ║
║ ❌ "Copy" → ✅ "Theek" or just a NOD (no words!)                  ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ Q2: Does each character have a UNIQUE voice?                 ║
║ ❌ Both say formal lines → ✅ Zain serious, Hamza casual          ║
║ Zain: "Dekh, seedha point pe aata hoon"                      ║
║ Hamza: "Haan bhai, bol na"                                   ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ Q3: Is it FREE of military/spy clichés?                      ║
║ ❌ "Roger", "Copy", "Target confirmed", "Mission accomplished"║
║ ✅ "Theek hai", "Hmm", "Chal", "Samjha"                         ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ Q4: Is it FREE of fake philosophy?                           ║
║ ❌ "Waqt sabka aata hai", "Manzil door nahi"                    ║
║ ✅ Just say what you mean, directly!                            ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ Q5: Can some lines be SILENT instead?                        ║
║ ❌ "Samajh gaya" (words) → ✅ Just a nod (visual)                 ║
║ ❌ "Ready?" "Haan" → ✅ Eye contact + nod (no words)              ║
╚═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

If ANY answer is NO → REWRITE the dialogue!



❌ vs ✅ DIALOGUE COMPARISON
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
❌ GARBAGE DIALOGUE (Filmy Syndrome):
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

HAMZA: "Plan?"
ZAIN: "Simple nahi. Seedha zaroor."
HAMZA: "Copy."

(later)
ZAIN: "Do choke points. Sitarah = route change. Chaand = hide."
HAMZA: "Samajh gaya."

(later)
HAMZA: "Ready?"
ZAIN: "Ilm amanat hai. Ready rehna parta hai."

PROBLEMS:
├── "Copy" = Who talks like this? Military movie!
├── "Samajh gaya" = Generic acknowledgment
├── "Simple nahi. Seedha zaroor" = Trying to sound cool
├── "Ilm amanat hai" = Fake deep (cringe!)
├── Both sound like ROBOTS, not friends
└── Zero personality difference!

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
✅ NATURAL DIALOGUE (Real Conversation):
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

HAMZA: "Yaar, subah subah... kya scene hai?"
ZAIN: "Dekh, seedha point pe aata hoon. Professor ka khat mila."
HAMZA: "Kaun professor?"
ZAIN: "Raza Mehdi. Kashmir wala cipher... uska pata chal gaya."
HAMZA: (eyebrows up, leans in) "Serious?"
ZAIN: (nods) "Aaj nikalna hai. Islamabad via, phir Muzaffarabad."
HAMZA: (thinking, then) "Chal, theek hai. Kab nikalein?"
ZAIN: "Ek ghante mein."
(beat - both look at each other)
HAMZA: (small smile) "Meri bike chalegi?"
ZAIN: (slight smile back) "Teri hi chalani hai."

WHAT'S BETTER:
├── "Yaar, subah subah" = How real friends greet
├── "Kaun professor?" = Natural question
├── "Serious?" = Real reaction, not "Copy"
├── Character voices DIFFERENT (Hamza casual, Zain focused)
├── "Meri bike chalegi?" = Personality moment!
├── Some beats are SILENT (just looks, nods)
└── Sounds like REAL conversation!

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════



BANNED DIALOGUE PHRASES - NEVER USE!
╔═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║ ❌ MILITARY/SPY CLICHÉS:                                         ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ • "Copy" / "Roger" / "Affirmative"                           ║
║ • "Target confirmed" / "Mission accomplished"                  ║
║ • "Move out" / "Let's roll"                                  ║
║ • "We have a situation"                                      ║
║ • "Go dark" / "Radio silence"                                ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ ❌ FAKE PHILOSOPHY:                                              ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ • "Ilm amanat hai"                                           ║
║ • "Waqt sabka aata hai"                                      ║
║ • "Manzil door nahi, raasta mushkil hai"                     ║
║ • "Zindagi mein kuch paane ke liye..."                       ║
║ • "Himmat aur hausla..."                                     ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ ❌ GENERIC RESPONSES:                                            ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║ • "Samajh gaya" (use nod instead!)                           ║
║ • "Theek hai, samjha" (use "Hmm" or silence!)                ║
║ • "Plan clear hai"                                           ║
║ • "Ready?" "Ready." (use eye contact instead!)               ║
╚═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝



CHARACTER VOICE DIFFERENTIATION
Each character MUST have a DISTINCT way of speaking:

EXAMPLE - Two Friends:

ZAIN (Protagonist - serious, focused):
├── Shorter sentences
├── Gets to the point
├── Doesn't joke much
├── "Dekh, seedha baat hai..."
├── "Nahi, yeh nahi chalega."
└── "Chal, time nahi hai."

HAMZA (Friend - lighter, warmer):
├── More casual language
├── Asks questions
├── Lightens mood sometimes
├── "Yaar, kya scene hai?"
├── "Arre serious? Phir toh..."
├── "Chal bhai, tera saath hai."

THEY SHOULD NOT SOUND THE SAME!



SILENT BEATS - USE THEM!
NOT every moment needs dialogue!

❌ TOO MUCH DIALOGUE:
Clip 2.3: Dialogue
Clip 2.4: Dialogue
Clip 2.5: Dialogue
Clip 2.6: Dialogue
Clip 2.7: Dialogue
Clip 2.8: Dialogue

✅ NATURAL MIX:
Clip 2.3: Dialogue (question + answer)
Clip 2.4: SILENT (thinking, looking at map)
Clip 2.5: Dialogue (important info)
Clip 2.6: SILENT (reaction, nod)
Clip 2.7: Dialogue (decision)
Clip 2.8: SILENT (shared look, understanding)

WHEN TO USE SILENCE:
├── After important information (let it land)
├── Emotional moments (looks > words)
├── Agreement/understanding (nod instead of "Samajh gaya")
├── Tension moments (silence builds suspense)
└── Decision points (character thinks before speaking)
DIALOGUE SCENE BALANCE
TYPICAL DIALOGUE SCENE (6-8 clips):

Clip 1: Dialogue (opener)
Clip 2: Dialogue (response)
Clip 3: SILENT (reaction beat)
Clip 4: Dialogue (key information)
Clip 5: SILENT (processing)
Clip 6: Dialogue (decision/conclusion)
Clip 7: SILENT (shared look)
Clip 8: Dialogue OR Silent (depends on scene)

RATIO: 50-60% dialogue clips, 40-50% silent beats
NOT: 100% dialogue in every clip!



DIALOGUE TEMPLATE
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
🗣️ DIALOGUE - Clip [X.X]
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

CHARACTER: [Name]
STYLE: [Emotion, delivery - natural, not theatrical]

LINE: "[Natural dialogue - passes 5 Question Test]"

TIMING: [When in clip]

✅ QUALITY CHECK:
├── Real person would say this? [YES/NO]
├── Character's unique voice? [YES/NO]
├── No military clichés? [YES/NO]
├── No fake philosophy? [YES/NO]
└── Could this be SILENT instead? [Consider it!]

════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════



STYLE for Dialogue:
Delivery Options:
├── Whispered, secretive
├── Normal conversation
├── Raised voice, urgent
├── Shouting, desperate
├── Muttering, to self
├── Questioning, uncertain
├── Commanding, authoritative
└── Emotional, breaking voice

Emotion Options:
├── Curious, intrigued
├── Worried, anxious
├── Determined, resolute
├── Scared, trembling
├── Angry, frustrated
├── Sad, grieving
├── Excited, discovering
├── Calm, reassuring
└── Suspicious, wary



DIALOGUE Examples (Natural, not Filmy):
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

DIALOGUE (HAMZA):
STYLE: Low voice, looking around
TEXT: "Yaar, yeh jagah... kuch ajeeb lag rahi hai."
TIMING: 0:01 - 0:03

[BEAT 0:03-0:04: Zain looks around, checking]

DIALOGUE (ZAIN):
STYLE: Calm but alert
TEXT: "Hmm. Chal, jaldi nikalte hain."
TIMING: 0:04 - 0:06

════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

DIALOGUE (HAMZA):
STYLE: Uncertain, looking at path
TEXT: "Yaar, sahi raasta hai na?"
TIMING: 0:00 - 0:02

DIALOGUE (ZAIN):
STYLE: Checking map, focused
TEXT: "Hona chahiye. Yeh turn lena hai."
TIMING: 0:02 - 0:04

[SILENT BEAT 0:04-0:05: Hamza nods, both look ahead]
DIALOGUE (HAMZA):
STYLE: Deep breath, ready
TEXT: "Chal."
TIMING: 0:05 - 0:06

════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

3️⃣ BACKGROUND MUSIC FORMAT

⚠️ CRITICAL: BG Music is NOT per clip - it covers MULTIPLE clips!
❌ WRONG (Clip by clip):
Clip 1.1 - Music A
Clip 1.2 - Music B
Clip 1.3 - Music C
(Sounds choppy, unprofessional, jarring!)

✅ CORRECT (Cinematic - like real movies):
Clips 1.1, 1.2, 1.3, 1.4 - ONE continuous music track (24 sec)
Clips 1.5, 1.6, 1.7 - ONE different track (18 sec)

BG Music should cover 3-6 clips minimum (18-36 seconds)

⚠️ IMPORTANT: Provide EXACT track names from royalty-free websites!
❌ WRONG (Vague descriptions - user can't find!):
"mysterious cinematic ambient, soft pads + subtle strings,
slow tempo (70–85 BPM), hint of adventure"
(User cannot search this!)

✅ CORRECT (Exact track names with source):
"Valley Sunset" by Alejandro Magaña (A. M.)
on https://mixkit.co/free-stock-music/

(User can directly find and download!)

**When BG Music is NOT needed:**

NO MUSIC for: ├── Clips with heavy dialogue (music distracts) ├── Quiet emotional
moments (silence is powerful) ├── Jump scare setups (silence before impact) ├──
Documentary-style talking segments └── When sound effects need to dominate

### 4⃣ SOUND EFFECTS FORMAT

**Sound effects add realism - use royalty-free sources with EXACT names!**

**⚠️ IMPORTANT: Provide EXACT sound effect names from royalty-free
websites!**

❌ WRONG (Vague - user can’t find!): “Morning ambience (very light): distant birds (0:00–
0:06), faint far traffic hum (very low, 0:00–0:06)” (User cannot search this!)

✅ CORRECT (Exact names with source): EFFECT: “Crickets and insects in the wild
ambience” Source: https://mixkit.co/free-sound-effects/ Timing: 0:00 - 0:06
(User can directly find and download!)

### 6⃣ SCENE-LEVEL AUDIO PLANNING
**⚠️ PLAN AUDIO AT SCENE LEVEL, NOT CLIP LEVEL!**

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════ 📋 SCENE 3: Study Room Discovery - AUDIO PLAN
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════
TOTAL CLIPS: 3.1 - 3.8 (8 clips = 48 seconds)

🎶 BACKGROUND MUSIC (Multi-clip blocks): ├── Clips 3.1-3.4: ONE mysterious ambient
track (24 sec) ├── Clips 3.5-3.8: ONE building tension track (24 sec) └── NOT separate
music per clip!

🎙️ NARRATION (Multi-clip flow): ├── Clips 3.1-3.3: ONE opening narration (18 sec) │ “Is
kamre mein 40 saal se koi nahi aaya tha… │ Dhool ki tehein waqt ki gawahi de rahi thi. │
Lekin Zain jaanta tha, yahan kuch hai jo │ uske dada ne chhupaya tha…” │ ├── Clips 3.4-
3.5: SILENT (let visuals breathe - discovery moment!) │ ├── Clip 3.6-3.7: Short revelation
narration (12 sec) │ “Aur phir usne dekha… woh kitaab jo 40 saal │ se is haveli ka sabse
gehra raaz thi.” │ └── Clip 3.8: SILENT (emotional moment, music only)

🗣️ DIALOGUE: ├── Clip 3.5: Zain whispers “Yeh hai…” (short) ├── Clip 3.7: Zain reads
aloud (brief) └── Most clips: NO dialogue (discovery is visual)

🔊 SOUND EFFECTS (Per clip as needed): ├── 3.1: Door creak, footsteps ├── 3.2: Room
ambience, dust settling ├── 3.3: Footsteps on wood ├── 3.4: Book sliding out, dust puff
├── 3.5: Book opening, soft gasp ├── 3.6: Page turning ├── 3.7: Paper rustle ├── 3.8:
Book closing, determined footsteps
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════

---

### 7⃣ NARRATION vs SILENCE GUIDE

**Not every clip needs narration! Strategic silence is powerful.**

WHEN TO USE NARRATION: ├── Scene openings (set the mood) ├── Time jumps (“Do
saal baad…”) ├── Inner thoughts (what character feels) ├── Mystery building (hint at
secrets) ├── Emotional context (why this matters) └── Story bridges (connecting scenes)
WHEN TO USE SILENCE (No narration): ├── Action moments (let visuals speak) ├──
Dialogue scenes (characters talking) ├── Discovery/reveal moments (audience needs to
absorb) ├── Tension peaks (silence builds suspense) ├── Emotional climaxes (music +
visuals only) ├── Visual beauty shots (let audience appreciate) └── After important
dialogue (let it land)
RATIO GUIDE: ├── 60-70% of clips: SILENT or dialogue only ├── 30-40% of clips:
Narration └── NOT every clip needs voiceover!

---

**THE PROBLEM:**
Technical prompts become SOULLESS - all camera movements, locks, and specs
but NO STORY!

❌ CURRENT ISSUE:

Prompt has: ├── Camera position ✓ ├── Visual continuity ✓ ├── Object lock ✓ ├──
Technical specs ✓ └── NARRATION? ❌ MISSING! STORY HOOK? ❌ MISSING! EMOTION?
❌ MISSING!
Result: Technically perfect but BORING video! Audience: “Kya dekh raha hoon? Kyun dekh
raha hoon?”

---

**SOLUTION: Every Clip Must Have STORY PURPOSE!**

**Three Questions for EVERY clip:**

Before writing any prompt, answer:
1⃣ WHAT is happening? (Action/Event) 2⃣ WHY does audience care? (Emotional hook) 3⃣
WHAT are they hearing? (Narration/Dialogue)
If you can’t answer all three → Clip needs work!

---

### NARRATION QUALITY RULES:

**Narration should be:**

✅ GOOD NARRATION: ├── INTRIGUING - Makes audience curious ├── EMOTIONAL -
Creates feeling ├── SPECIFIC - Not generic statements ├── POETIC - Beautiful language
(for Urdu) ├── CONNECTED - Links to visuals on screen └── PURPOSEFUL - Moves story
forward

❌ BAD NARRATION: ├── GENERIC - “Yeh ek gali thi” (So what?) ├── OBVIOUS -
Describing what we already see ├── FLAT - No emotion, no rhythm ├── DISCONNECTED -
Doesn’t match visuals ├── FILLER - Words without meaning └── BORING - Doesn’t make
audience curious
---

**NARRATION TYPES:**

  1.   HOOK NARRATION (Opening) Purpose: Grab attention, create curiosity Example:
       “Kuch kahaniyan sunayi nahi jaatiin… wo mehsoos ki jaati hain.”

  2.   SCENE-SETTING NARRATION Purpose: Establish mood, place, time Example:
       “Faisalabad ki in purani galiyon mein waqt thehar sa gaya tha…”

  3.   CHARACTER NARRATION Purpose: Introduce/reveal character Example: “Zain ne
       kabhi socha nahi tha ke ek kitaab uski zindagi badal degi.”

  4.   MYSTERY/TENSION NARRATION Purpose: Build suspense, hint at secrets Example:
       “Is band dukaan ke peeche kuch tha… jo 40 saal se intezaar kar raha tha.”

  5.   EMOTIONAL NARRATION Purpose: Create feeling, connect hearts Example: “Dada ki
       wo aakhri mulaqaat… aaj bhi aankhein nam kar deti hai.”

  6.   TRANSITION NARRATION Purpose: Time jump, location change Example: “Do saal
       guzar gaye… lekin sawaal wahi tha.”


---

**NARRATION FORMULA:**

ENGAGING NARRATION = VISUAL ANCHOR + EMOTIONAL HOOK + CURIOSITY ELEMENT
VISUAL ANCHOR: What we’re seeing EMOTIONAL HOOK: Why we should feel something
CURIOSITY ELEMENT: What makes us want more

**Example:**

❌ FLAT/BORING: “Yeh Faisalabad ki ek purani gali thi.” (Generic, no emotion, no
curiosity)

✅ ENGAGING: “Faisalabad ki is gali mein ek dukaan thi… jiska shutter 40 saal se nahi
khula tha. Koi nahi jaanta tha kyun.”
Visual anchor: Gali, dukaan Emotional hook: 40 saal (long time = mystery) Curiosity: Kyun
nahi khula? What’s inside?

---

**THE BOOKSHOP LANE - IMPROVED:**
❌ ORIGINAL (No narration, just technical): “Camera continues slow forward drift and
subtly pans right to bring several old shuttered shopfronts into view. One specific shop has
a fully closed metal shutter and a small, aged signboard above it…”

✅ IMPROVED (Story + Technical): “Camera continues slow forward drift and subtly pans
right to bring several old shuttered shopfronts into view.
NARRATION (Deep male voice, thoughtful, Roman Urdu): ’Is gali ki her dukaan ki apni
kahani thi… lekin ek dukaan aisi thi jiska shutter chaar dashak se band tha.
Log kehte the dukandaar ek raat ghayab ho gaya… kitaabon ke saath.’
The narration plays as camera reveals the specific shuttered bookshop - aged signboard
with faded lettering, metal shutter firmly closed, decades of dust.“

---

### DIALOGUE QUALITY RULES:

**Dialogue should be:**

✅ GOOD DIALOGUE: ├── NATURAL - How people actually talk ├── CHARACTER-
SPECIFIC - Each person sounds different ├── SUBTEXT - Not everything said directly ├──
PURPOSEFUL - Reveals character or moves plot ├── MEMORABLE - Lines that stick └──
CONVERSATIONAL - Not formal/stiff

❌ BAD DIALOGUE: ├── EXPOSITION DUMP - “Jaise tum jaante ho, yeh kitaab…” ├── ON-
THE-NOSE - Saying exactly what they feel ├── GENERIC - Anyone could say it ├──
FORMAL - Too proper, not natural ├── MEANINGLESS - Words without purpose └──
SAME VOICE - Everyone sounds identical

---

**DIALOGUE EXAMPLES:**

❌ BAD (Exposition, unnatural): ZAIN: “Yeh kitaab mere dada ki hai jo 40 saal pehle
ghayab ho gaye the aur maine yeh unke kamre mein ek hidden jagah se dhoondi.”
(No one talks like this! Too much info at once!)

✅ GOOD (Natural, character-driven): HAMZA: “Yaar, yeh kitaab… kaafi purani lagti hai.”
ZAIN: (quietly) “Dada ki thi.” HAMZA: “Dada? Woh jo…” ZAIN: (cutting off) “Haan. Wahi.”
(pause) HAMZA: “Tujhe kahan mili?” ZAIN: “Woh… complicated hai.”
(Natural pauses, incomplete sentences, subtext!)

---
**CHARACTER VOICE DIFFERENTIATION:**

Each character should have UNIQUE speech pattern:
ZAIN (Protagonist - thoughtful, educated): ├── Speaks: Measured, thoughtful pauses ├──
Words: Mix of Urdu/English, educated vocabulary ├── Phrases: “Mujhe lagta hai…”,
“Shayad…” └── Style: Doesn’t say everything, holds back
HAMZA (Friend - casual, loyal): ├── Speaks: Quick, reactive, casual ├── Words: Street-
smart, friendly slang ├── Phrases: “Yaar”, “Chal na”, “Kya scene hai?” └── Style: Says what
he thinks, no filter
DADA (Grandfather - wise, mysterious): ├── Speaks: Slow, deliberate, meaningful ├──
Words: Classic Urdu, poetic ├── Phrases: “Beta…”, “Yaad rakhna…” └── Style: Every word
has weight
VILLAIN (Naeem - calculated, threatening): ├── Speaks: Controlled, soft but menacing
├── Words: Formal, cold ├── Phrases: “Dekhiye…”, “Aap samajh nahi rahe…” └── Style:
Threat hidden in politeness

---

### NARRATION/DIALOGUE TIMING:

**Match words to visuals:**

RULE: Narration should COMPLEMENT visuals, not compete!
When to narrate: ├── During SLOW camera movements (dolly, pan) ├── During
ESTABLISHING shots ├── During EMOTIONAL moments ├── During TIME transitions
When NOT to narrate: ├── During FAST action ├── During DIALOGUE scenes ├── When
visuals speak for themselves ├── During REVEAL moments (let audience see first)

---

### STORY INTEGRATION TEMPLATE:

**Every video prompt should include:**

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════ 📖 STORY ELEMENT:
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════
CLIP PURPOSE: Why does this clip exist in the story? [Answer: What does audience
learn/feel from this clip?]
NARRATION/DIALOGUE: [Full text in Roman Urdu] [Voice direction: tone, pace, emotion]
[Timing: when in clip does it play?]
VISUAL-AUDIO SYNC: [What is audience SEEING while HEARING the narration?] [Do they
complement each other?]
EMOTIONAL BEAT: [What should audience FEEL during this clip?] [Curious? Scared? Sad?
Excited?]
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════

---

### COMPLETE EXAMPLE - Bookshop Lane:

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════

═════

📖 STORY ELEMENT:
CLIP PURPOSE: Introduce the mysterious closed bookshop that will be central to the story.
Plant seeds of mystery.
NARRATION (Deep male voice, thoughtful, unhurried): “Is gali ki her dukaan ki apni kahani
thi… lekin ek dukaan aisi thi jiska shutter chaar dashak se band tha.
Log kehte the dukandaar ek raat ghayab ho gaya… kitaabon ke saath.“
Voice direction: Measured pace, slight pause after “band tha”, hint of mystery in voice on
“ghayab ho gaya”

  •    “ek dukaan aisi thi” → Camera finds the specific shop
  •    “shutter chaar dashak se band tha” → Close on rusted shutter

visible, shops ahead

lekin ek dukaan aisi thi jiska shutter chaar dashak se band tha.
Log kehte the dukandaar ek raat ghayab ho gaya… kitaabon ke saath.“
