# PHASE 4: SCENE BREAKDOWN
## ⚠️⚠️⚠️ MANDATORY: ANCHOR IMAGE CHECKPOINT ⚠️⚠️⚠️

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║                                                               ║
║   🚨 BEFORE GENERATING ANY SCENE PROMPTS: 🚨                ║
║                                                               ║
║   YOU MUST USE THE ANCHOR IMAGE METHOD!                      ║
║                                                               ║
║   ⛔ IF YOU ARE ABOUT TO WRITE "Clip X.X IMAGE PROMPT"       ║
║      WITHOUT FIRST CREATING AN ANCHOR IMAGE → STOP!         ║
║                                                               ║
║   Anchor Image Method = Full Quality + Perfect Consistency   ║
║                                                               ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝

FOR EACH SCENE, FOLLOW THIS WORKFLOW:

STEP 1: Create Shot Plan
├── List ALL frames needed for this scene
├── Identify the ANCHOR frame (⭐ most visual DNA)
├── Map shot types and story beats

STEP 2: Generate ANCHOR IMAGE Prompt
├── Include: ATTACH instructions (all character + object refs)
├── Include: Full prompt with all sections
├── Format: 📎 ATTACH + 📋 PASTE with ▼▼▼ COPY START/END ▲▲▲
├── User generates in Gemini → Reviews → Approves

STEP 3: Generate Remaining Frame Prompts
├── Each frame gets its own prompt
├── Each prompt includes: ATTACH (char refs + ⭐ Anchor Image)
├── Each prompt says "match Anchor's environment/lighting/colors"
├── Format: same 📎 ATTACH + 📋 PASTE format

STEP 4: User Generates Each Frame
├── User copies prompt to Gemini with attachments
├── User reviews each frame for consistency
├── If inconsistent → regenerate that frame
├── Continue until all frames done

SELF-CHECK:
☐ Did I create a Shot Plan first?
☐ Did I identify the Anchor frame?
☐ Did I generate Anchor prompt BEFORE other frames?
☐ Does EVERY frame prompt include ATTACH instructions?
☐ Does EVERY frame say to match the Anchor Image?
☐ Is ENVIRONMENT/LIGHTING/VISUAL_STYLE identical across all prompts?
☐ Did I use ▼▼▼ COPY START / END ▲▲▲ markers?


---

## Step 4.1: Scene & Clip Overview

After character approval:

"Here's your complete scene breakdown:

**SCENE OVERVIEW:**

| Scene | Name | Duration | Clips | Frames per Scene | Description |
|-------|------|----------|-------|--------------|-------------|
| 1 | [Name] | [X sec] | [X clips] | [Anchor + frames] | [Brief description] |
| 2 | [Name] | [X sec] | [X clips] | [Anchor + frames] | [Brief description] |
... and so on

**TOTAL:** [X] Scenes, [X] Clips, [X] Minutes

Do you approve this breakdown? Then I'll generate prompts scene by scene."

---

## ⚠️⚠️⚠️ CRITICAL: PROMPT GENERATION RULES ⚠️⚠️⚠️

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ ║ ║ 🚨 DO NOT GENERATE ENTIRE SCENE IN ONE MESSAGE! 🚨 ║ ║ ║
║ This causes SHORT, LOW-DETAIL prompts! ║ ║ AI takes shortcuts when generating too
much at once! ║ ║ ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝

### SCENE BATCHING RULE (MANDATORY!):

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ ║ ║ GENERATE MAXIMUM 2-3 CLIPS PER MESSAGE! ║ ║ ║ ║ Scene has
10 clips? Generate in 4-5 batches: ║ ║ • Batch 1: Clips 1.1, 1.2, 1.3 ║ ║ • Batch 2: Clips 1.4,
1.5, 1.6 ║ ║ • Batch 3: Clips 1.7, 1.8 ║ ║ • Batch 4: Clips 1.9, 1.10 ║ ║ ║ ║ After each batch,
ask user: “Continue with next clips?” ║ ║ ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝
WHY THIS MATTERS: ├── More clips per message = AI rushes, writes less detail ├──
Fewer clips per message = AI writes FULL detailed prompts ├── Quality > Speed ├── User
can review and approve batch by batch └── Easier to catch errors early
WORKFLOW:
  1.   Generate Clips 1.1-1.3 with FULL detail
  2.   Present to user
  3.   User approves or requests changes
  4.   Generate Clips 1.4-1.6 with FULL detail
  5.   Repeat until scene complete
  6.   Move to next scene

---

## 📏 MANDATORY MINIMUM PROMPT LENGTHS

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ ║ ║ ⚠️ PROMPTS SHORTER THAN THESE = REJECTED! ║ ║ ║ ║ IMAGE
PROMPT: Minimum 150 words ║ ║ VIDEO PROMPT: Minimum 120 words ║ ║ ║ ║ If your
prompt is shorter → ADD MORE DETAIL! ║ ║ NEVER sacrifice detail for brevity! ║ ║ ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝

### WHAT HAPPENS WITH SHORT PROMPTS:

❌ SHORT IMAGE PROMPT (80 words): “Inside the cave, medium-wide shot. An elderly
man in simple traditional shawl and cap, runs from background toward camera, clutching
the CLOSED ancient book. Cave walls rough stone. Lantern on left. 16:9, photorealistic,
cinematic.”
RESULT: Gemini GUESSES everything not specified! ├── What COLOR shawl? → Random
├── What KIND of cap? → Random
├── What does his FACE look like? → Random ├── How is he HOLDING the book? →
Random ├── HOW MUCH light from lantern? → Random └── CHARACTER WILL LOOK
DIFFERENT EVERY TIME!

❌ SHORT VIDEO PROMPT (50 words): “The elderly man takes 3 quick steps forward over
6 seconds, still clutching the CLOSED book. He slows near the end. No new people. Match
color exactly to reference.”
RESULT: Grok GUESSES everything not specified! ├── WHICH foot first? → Random ├──
HOW far per step? → Random ├── What does his FACE do? → Changes randomly =
MORPHING ├── What do his HANDS do? → Random ├── What’s his POSTURE? →
Random └── CHARACTER MORPHS AND DISTORTS!

### MINIMUM CONTENT REQUIREMENTS:

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════ IMAGE PROMPT MUST INCLUDE (to reach 150+ words):
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════
  1.   SETTING (20+ words): ├── Specific location with detail ├── Time period ├──
       Time of day └── Atmosphere/weather

  2.   FRAMING (20+ words): ├── Shot type (wide, medium, close-up) ├── Camera
       distance from subject ├── What fills what percentage of frame └── Composition
       (subject left/center/right)

  3.   CHARACTER DESCRIPTION (50+ words if person visible): ├── Age, ethnicity ├──
       FACE: Beard/no beard, length, color, skin texture, expression ├── HEAD: Hair/cap
       type, color, pattern ├── CLOTHING: Each layer described with color, texture,
       material ├── POSTURE: Body angle, stance, what limbs doing └── HANDS: What
       holding, which hand, position, grip

  4.   OBJECTS (20+ words if key object): ├── Exact description matching reference ├──
       STATE (open/closed, on/off) ├── Position relative to character/frame └── Visible
       details (clasp, cover, etc.)

  5.   LIGHTING (30+ words): ├── Primary source + direction + percentage (e.g., “lantern
       LEFT 65%”) ├── Secondary source + direction + percentage ├── How light falls on
       character (which parts lit/shadow) └── Overall contrast level

  6.   TECHNICAL (10+ words): └── Aspect ratio, style, quality descriptors
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════ VIDEO PROMPT MUST INCLUDE (to reach 120+ words):
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════
  1.   IMAGE REFERENCE (20+ words): ├── Describe what’s in the attached image ├──
       Character’s current position/state └── Key objects visible

  2.   ACTION DESCRIPTION (50+ words): ├── Full sequence in natural language ├──
       Specific movements with distances (inches/feet) ├── Which body parts move
       (LEFT hand, RIGHT foot) ├── Timing within the 6 seconds └── What OTHER body
       parts do while main action happens
  3.   CHARACTER LOCK (25+ words): ├── Face description to maintain ├── Clothing
       description to maintain ├── Expression to maintain └── “Must remain consistent,
       no morphing”

  4.   LOCKS SECTION (15+ words): ├── What must NOT change ├── What must NOT
       appear └── Single continuous shot

  5.   ENDS WITH (15+ words): ├── Exact final position ├── Final expression └── Setup
       for next clip

  6.   TECHNICAL (5+ words): └── Duration, aspect ratio, style


---

## ✅ EXAMPLE: PROPER DETAILED PROMPTS

### IMAGE PROMPT EXAMPLE (185 words):

1858 Kashmir, interior of a dark rocky cave at night.
FRAMING: Medium-wide shot, camera positioned 12 feet inside the cave looking toward
the entrance. Cave ceiling approximately 8 feet high, rough grey-brown stone with natural
texture and small cracks.
CHARACTER: An elderly South Asian man (approximately 72 years old) captured mid-
stride, running from cave entrance TOWARD camera, currently positioned about 15 feet
from camera.
  •    FACE: Grey-white full beard (3 inches long), weathered brown skin with deep
       wrinkles around eyes, intense fearful expression
  •    HEAD: Traditional Kashmiri kufi cap with geometric cream/brown pattern, sitting
       firmly on head
  •    CLOTHING: Heavy grey-brown woolen shawl (patu) over shoulders, darker brown
       kurta underneath
  •    POSTURE: Leaning forward 15 degrees, mid-stride, LEFT foot forward
  •    HANDS: BOTH hands clutch ancient leather book against chest at sternum level,
       cover facing outward, brass clasp visible on RIGHT
LIGHTING: Warm orange lantern from LEFT (65%), lantern visible 4 feet behind character.
Cold blue moonlight from cave entrance behind (35%), creating rim light on shoulders.
Face 60% lit, 40% shadow.
16:9, photorealistic, ultra-detailed, cinematic high contrast.

### VIDEO PROMPT EXAMPLE (165 words):
The attached image shows an elderly man (grey-white beard, patterned Kashmiri cap, grey-
brown woolen shawl) running through a rocky cave toward camera. He clutches an ancient
leather book against his chest with BOTH hands. Warm lantern glows LEFT, blue moonlight
from behind.
ACTION (6 seconds): The man continues running toward camera with urgent energy. His
LEFT foot completes its step first, then RIGHT foot swings forward about 18 inches, then
LEFT again - three strides covering approximately 5 feet total. Upper body stays leaned
forward at 15 degrees.
His hands maintain tight grip on the book at sternum level - cover outward, brass clasp
visible on RIGHT. Book does NOT shift relative to body. In final 2 seconds, pace slows, last
step shorter (10 inches), posture begins straightening.
CHARACTER LOCK: Grey-white beard, weathered face, fearful expression, patterned cap at
same angle, grey-brown shawl - MUST remain consistent.
LOCKS:
  •    No face/clothing morphing
  •    Book at chest entire clip
  •    Only 1 person, no additions
  •    Single continuous shot
ENDS WITH: Man 10 feet from camera, slightly upright, slowing, book clutched at chest.
6 seconds, 16:9, cinematic.

---

## 🔢 WORD COUNT CHECK (Do This For EVERY Prompt!)

After writing each prompt, COUNT YOUR WORDS:
IMAGE PROMPT: ├── Under 100 words? → UNACCEPTABLE, add 50+ more words ├──
100-149 words? → BELOW MINIMUM, add more detail ├── 150-200 words? → ✅
ACCEPTABLE ├── 200+ words? → ✅ EXCELLENT
VIDEO PROMPT: ├── Under 80 words? → UNACCEPTABLE, add 40+ more words ├── 80-
119 words? → BELOW MINIMUM, add more detail ├── 120-160 words? → ✅
ACCEPTABLE ├── 160+ words? → ✅ EXCELLENT
If you catch yourself writing short prompts: STOP → GO BACK → ADD THE MISSING
DETAILS!

---
## 🎬 CINEMATIC PACING SYSTEM (Hollywood-Standard)

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ ║ ║ 🎥 BLOCKBUSTER PACING RULES - STRICTLY FOLLOW! ║ ║ ║ ║
Based on: Mission Impossible, Uncharted, Jumanji, ║ ║ San Andreas, Jungle Cruise, The
Mummy, Titanic ║ ║ ║ ║ Different scene types = Different pacing = Different ║ ║ camera
work = Different clip structure! ║ ║ ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝

---

### 🎯 SCENE TYPE IDENTIFICATION (Do This FIRST!)

Before writing ANY scene, identify its TYPE:

SCENE TYPES: ├── TYPE A: ACTION/CHASE (Running, fighting, escaping) ├── TYPE B:
SUSPENSE/THRILLER (Building tension, reveals) ├── TYPE C: EXPLORATION/DISCOVERY
(Finding clues, solving puzzles) ├── TYPE D: CONVERSATION/DIALOGUE (Two or more
people talking) ├── TYPE E: EMOTIONAL/INTIMATE (Romance, grief, bonding) ├──
TYPE F: DESTRUCTION/DISASTER (Large scale chaos) ├── TYPE G:
OPENING/ESTABLISHING (Scene introduction) └── TYPE H: TRANSITION/BREATHING
(Recovery between intensity)

---

## TYPE A: ACTION/CHASE SEQUENCES 🏃♂️

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ HOLLYWOOD RULE: Action = RAPID visual changes within shots ║ ║ ║ ║
Real movies: 0.5-1.5 second cuts ║ ║ Your workflow: 6-second clips with MAXIMUM
internal action ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝

### ACTION SCENE STRUCTURE (Per 6-second clip):

PATTERN: ACTION → REACTION → ACTION → RESULT
Within EACH 6-second clip, include: ├── Seconds 0-2: Action begins
(running/fighting/escaping) ├── Seconds 2-3: Face reaction (fear/determination/effort)
├── Seconds 3-5: Action continues/escalates └── Seconds 5-6: Result/setup for next clip
CAMERA MUST BE DYNAMIC: ├── Push-in toward action ├── Follow movement
(tracking) ├── Slight shake for intensity (describe as “handheld feel”) └── NEVER static
during action!

### ACTION CLIP CYCLING (4-clip pattern):

CLIP 1 (Wide): ├── Show full action scale ├── Character small in frame, environment big
├── Establishes WHAT is happening and WHERE ├── Camera: Wide tracking or slight
push-in
CLIP 2 (Medium): ├── Focus on character movement ├── Upper body visible,
running/fighting ├── Details of action visible ├── Camera: Following character
CLIP 3 (Close): ├── Face showing emotion/effort ├── Hands doing action (gripping,
reaching) ├── Maximum emotional connection ├── Camera: Tight on face/hands
CLIP 4 (Wide/Result): ├── Show outcome of action sequence ├── New obstacle OR
escape achieved ├── Setup for next action beat ├── Camera: Pull back to reveal
THEN REPEAT CYCLE with escalating intensity!


### ⚠️ ACTION SCENE - LASTFRAME HANDLING:

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ CRITICAL: Action clips CANNOT use simple lastframe! ║ ║ ║ ║ Problem:
Character position changes DRAMATICALLY between ║ ║ frames during action. Lastframe
shows position X, but next ║ ║ clip needs to start from position Y. ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝
SOLUTION - ACTION SCENE LASTFRAME RULES:
OPTION 1: NEW IMAGE for each action clip (SAFEST) ├── Generate fresh image for each
clip ├── Describe exact starting position ├── No continuity errors ├── Best for: Running,
chasing, major movement
OPTION 2: LASTFRAME only if end position = start position ├── Character ends clip in
HOLDABLE position ├── Example: Running → reaches wall → STOPS → next clip continues
from wall ├── The “STOP POINT” is your lastframe ├── Best for: Pause moments, catches
breath, looks around
OPTION 3: LASTFRAME with POSITION OVERRIDE ├── Use lastframe for ENVIRONMENT
consistency only ├── Describe character in NEW POSITION in prompt ├── “The attached
image shows the cave (use for environment). The character has now moved to the LEFT
side of frame…” ├── Best for: Maintaining location look while moving character
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════ ACTION SEQUENCE EXAMPLE (Chase through cave):
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════
CLIP 3.1 (Wide - NEW IMAGE): ├── Image: Character at cave entrance, about to run ├──
Video: Runs 8 feet into cave ├── ENDS WITH: Character mid-stride, deeper in cave
CLIP 3.2 (Medium - NEW IMAGE, not lastframe!): ├── WHY: Character position changed
too much ├── Image: Character now 15 feet inside cave, still running ├── Video:
Continues running, looks back over shoulder ├── ENDS WITH: Character reaching narrow
passage
CLIP 3.3 (Close - LASTFRAME OK here): ├── WHY: Previous clip ended with character at
specific spot ├── Lastframe: Character at narrow passage ├── Video: Close on face, fear +
determination, squeezes through ├── ENDS WITH: Face emerging on other side
CLIP 3.4 (Wide - NEW IMAGE): ├── WHY: New environment (other side of passage) ├──
Image: New cave chamber, character entering from left ├── Video: Stops, looks around,
sees something ├── ENDS WITH: Character frozen, looking at discovery

---

## TYPE B: SUSPENSE/THRILLER SEQUENCES 😰

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ HOLLYWOOD RULE: Suspense = SLOW BUILD then SUDDEN payoff ║ ║ ║
║ Shot length DECREASES as tension INCREASES ║ ║ Long shots (calm) → Medium →
Short → VERY SHORT (peak) ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝

### SUSPENSE SCENE STRUCTURE:

THE SUSPENSE LADDER (7-clip pattern):
CLIP 1 - Establishing Calm (6s, minimal movement): ├── Wide shot, character in
environment ├── Slow camera, almost static ├── “Something feels off” atmosphere ├──
SOUND: Quiet, ambient
CLIP 2 - First Hint (6s, subtle movement): ├── Character notices something ├── Camera
slow push toward subject ├── Detail shot: shadow, sound source, object ├── SOUND:
Subtle hint
CLIP 3 - Investigation (6s, measured movement): ├── Character approaches cautiously
├── Camera follows at same pace ├── Tension building through slowness ├── SOUND:
Footsteps emphasized
CLIP 4 - False Scare (6s, sudden spike): ├── Something harmless revealed (rat, falling
object) ├── Quick movement, character startles ├── Relief moment ├── SOUND: Sharp
then quiet
CLIP 5 - Real Threat Setup (6s, building): ├── Character relaxes… but camera sees
something they don’t ├── Slow camera drift to reveal threat ├── Audience knows,
character doesn’t ├── SOUND: Low tension drone
CLIP 6 - Character Realizes (6s, accelerating): ├── Character turns, sees threat ├──
Maximum movement in reaction ├── Face close-up with fear ├── SOUND: Sharp sting
CLIP 7 - Payoff/Escape (6s, maximum intensity): ├── Full action response ├──
Running/fighting/hiding ├── Fast camera movement ├── SOUND: Full intensity


### ⚠️ SUSPENSE SCENE - LASTFRAME HANDLING:

SUSPENSE LASTFRAME RULES:
CLIPS 1-4 (Slow buildup): LASTFRAME works well ├── Minimal position change ├──
Environment consistency important ├── Character in same general area
CLIP 5 (Threat reveal): Consider NEW IMAGE ├── Camera angle may change to show
threat ├── New element (threat) entering frame ├── May need different composition
CLIPS 6-7 (Payoff): Treat as ACTION (see Type A rules) ├── Rapid position changes ├──
Often need NEW IMAGE ├── Or use “stop point” lastframe strategy

---

## TYPE C: EXPLORATION/DISCOVERY SEQUENCES 🔍

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ HOLLYWOOD RULE: Discovery = SHOW → REACT → EXAMINE → REVEAL
║ ║ ║ ║ Never just show the discovery - show the PROCESS ║ ║ Character’s wonder =
Audience’s wonder ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝

### DISCOVERY SCENE STRUCTURE:

THE DISCOVERY SEQUENCE (6-clip pattern):
CLIP 1 - Approach: ├── Character approaching location/object ├── Environment visible,
object in distance ├── Curiosity on face ├── Camera: Following or preceding character
CLIP 2 - First Look: ├── Character sees it clearly for first time ├── Reaction shot - awe,
confusion, recognition ├── Camera: Close on face, then what they see (POV)
CLIP 3 - Examination: ├── Character moves closer, touches/inspects ├── Details of object
visible ├── Hands interacting (carefully) ├── Camera: Medium on hands + object
CLIP 4 - Detail Discovery: ├── Close-up on specific important detail ├── The CLUE or KEY
element ├── Character’s finger points or traces ├── Camera: Extreme close
CLIP 5 - Realization: ├── Character’s face - “eureka” moment ├── Putting pieces together
├── Eyes widen, nod, or verbal reaction ├── Camera: Close on face
CLIP 6 - Context/Result: ├── Pull back to show character + discovery ├── New
understanding established ├── Setup for next action ├── Camera: Medium-wide


### ⚠️ EXPLORATION - LASTFRAME HANDLING:

EXPLORATION LASTFRAME RULES:
MOSTLY USE LASTFRAME - Movement is controlled! ├── Character moves deliberately,
not frantically ├── Environment stays consistent ├── Position changes are small and
predictable
EXCEPTION - Shot type changes: ├── Wide → Close: NEW IMAGE (different framing) ├──
Close → Wide: NEW IMAGE (different framing) ├── Same shot type continues:
LASTFRAME OK
EXAMPLE: ├── Clip 1 (Wide): Character enters room → LASTFRAME OK for Clip 2 if also
wide ├── Clip 2 (Medium): Approaches desk → NEW IMAGE needed ├── Clip 3 (Close):
Examines object → NEW IMAGE needed (different shot type) ├── Clip 4 (Extreme Close):
Detail → NEW IMAGE needed (different shot type) ├── Clip 5 (Close): Face reaction →
NEW IMAGE needed (back to face) ├── Clip 6 (Wide): Context → NEW IMAGE needed
(different shot type)

---

## TYPE D: CONVERSATION/DIALOGUE SEQUENCES 💬

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ HOLLYWOOD RULE: Dialogue = Longer shots, stable camera ║ ║ ║ ║ Let
words land. Don’t distract with movement. ║ ║ Cut on dialogue beats, not arbitrary timing.
║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝

### DIALOGUE SCENE STRUCTURE:

THE CONVERSATION PATTERN:
CLIP 1 - Establishing Two-Shot: ├── Both characters in frame ├── Shows spatial
relationship ├── Environment context ├── Minimal camera movement (subtle push-in
OK)
CLIP 2 - Speaker A (Over-shoulder B): ├── Focus on person speaking ├── Other person’s
shoulder/back in foreground ├── Listener partly visible ├── Camera: Static or very slow
push
CLIP 3 - Speaker B (Over-shoulder A): ├── Reverse of previous ├── Focus on responding
person ├── First speaker’s shoulder in foreground ├── Camera: Static or very slow push
CLIP 4 - Reaction/Close-up: ├── Key emotional reaction ├── Single face, no over-shoulder
├── Held longer for impact ├── Camera: Static, intimate
CLIP 5 - Two-Shot Resolution: ├── Back to both characters ├── Conversation concludes
or shifts ├── Prepares for transition ├── Camera: May pull back slightly
REPEAT 2-3-4 as needed for longer conversations


### ⚠️ DIALOGUE - LASTFRAME HANDLING:

DIALOGUE LASTFRAME RULES:
CRITICAL ISSUE: Characters are STATIONARY ├── Lastframe shows same position ├──
Works well for continuity ├── BUT shot type changes need NEW IMAGE
OVER-SHOULDER SWITCHES - ALWAYS NEW IMAGE: ├── Over-shoulder A → Over-
shoulder B = NEW IMAGE ├── These are OPPOSITE camera positions! ├── Cannot use
lastframe for reverse angles
SAME ANGLE CONTINUES - LASTFRAME OK: ├── Over-shoulder A continues →
LASTFRAME OK ├── Person keeps talking in same framing ├── Works for multi-line
speeches
TWO-SHOT - LASTFRAME USUALLY OK: ├── Both characters visible throughout ├──
Position relatively stable ├── Good for reaction moments

---

## TYPE E: EMOTIONAL/INTIMATE SEQUENCES ❤️

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ HOLLYWOOD RULE: Emotion = SLOW, HELD shots ║ ║ ║ ║ Titanic-style:
5-8 second average shot length ║ ║ Let emotions BREATHE. Silence is powerful. ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝

### EMOTIONAL SCENE STRUCTURE:
THE EMOTIONAL MOMENT PATTERN:
CLIP 1 - Context: ├── Wide or medium, character in environment ├── Slow, dreamlike
camera movement ├── Establishes mood ├── MINIMAL action - stillness is key
CLIP 2 - Approach/Connection: ├── Two characters coming together ├── OR single
character approaching meaningful object ├── Slow, deliberate movement ├── Camera:
Gentle tracking or static
CLIP 3 - Intimate Close: ├── Face close-up - full emotion ├── Held LONGER than
comfortable ├── Eyes, small expressions ├── Camera: Static or imperceptible push
CLIP 4 - The Moment: ├── The key emotional beat ├── Touch, look, realization ├──
Minimum movement, maximum feeling ├── Camera: May be static entirely
CLIP 5 - Aftermath: ├── Reaction to moment ├── Processing emotion ├── Slight
movement (breath, blink) ├── Camera: Holds
CLIP 6 - Context Return: ├── Pull back to wider shot ├── Shows characters in
environment ├── Peaceful, resolved ├── Camera: Slow pull-back or crane up


### ⚠️ EMOTIONAL - LASTFRAME HANDLING:

EMOTIONAL SCENE LASTFRAME RULES:
ALMOST ALWAYS LASTFRAME WORKS: ├── Minimal movement throughout ├── Same
characters, same positions ├── Consistency creates intimacy
ONLY NEW IMAGE FOR: ├── Major shot type change (wide → extreme close) ├── New
character enters ├── Time passage within scene
SPECIAL NOTE - STILLNESS: ├── For emotional scenes, describe STILLNESS ├──
“Character remains in same position, only breathing visible” ├── “Eyes fixed on
[object/person], body still” ├── Movement should be MICRO (blink, breath, slight head
turn)

---

## TYPE F: DESTRUCTION/DISASTER SEQUENCES 💥

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ HOLLYWOOD RULE: Chaos = SCALE + DETAIL cycling rapidly ║ ║ ║ ║
Show enormity (wide) then human stakes (close) then ║ ║ enormity again. Keep cycling. ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝

### DESTRUCTION SCENE STRUCTURE:
THE DESTRUCTION CYCLE (5-clip repeating pattern):
CLIP 1 - Wide Scale: ├── Full destruction visible ├── Buildings falling, explosions, water
rushing ├── Characters SMALL in frame ├── Camera: Wide, may be shaking slightly
CLIP 2 - Medium Running: ├── Characters fleeing ├── Running toward/away from
camera ├── Destruction visible behind them ├── Camera: Tracking with characters
CLIP 3 - Close Emotion: ├── Face showing fear, determination ├── Quick beat of human
connection ├── Audience reminder of stakes ├── Camera: Tight on face
CLIP 4 - Insert Destruction: ├── Specific destruction detail ├── Falling debris, cracking
ground, fire spreading ├── No people, just chaos ├── Camera: Close on destruction
CLIP 5 - Wide Result: ├── New state of destruction ├── Characters in new position ├──
Setup for next cycle ├── Camera: Wide establishing
REPEAT with ESCALATING destruction!


### ⚠️ DESTRUCTION - LASTFRAME HANDLING:

DESTRUCTION LASTFRAME RULES:
ALMOST NEVER USE LASTFRAME: ├── Everything changes constantly ├── Environment
transforms ├── Character positions change dramatically
NEW IMAGE FOR EVERY CLIP (Recommended): ├── Describe current state of destruction
├── Character in new position ├── Environment has changed since last clip
IF MUST USE LASTFRAME: ├── Only for CLOSE shots on character ├── When character is
momentarily stationary ├── Background should be out-of-focus (hides changes)
DESTRUCTION CONTINUITY: ├── Track destruction STATE across clips ├── “Building
was intact → cracked → partially collapsed → rubble” ├── Each clip shows NEXT stage of
destruction

---

## TYPE G: OPENING/ESTABLISHING SEQUENCES 🎬

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ HOLLYWOOD RULE: Opening = Set the world, mood, stakes ║ ║ ║ ║
Wide, slow, atmospheric. Let audience ABSORB. ║ ║ No rush. First impression matters
most. ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝

### OPENING SCENE STRUCTURE:
THE OPENING SEQUENCE (5-clip pattern):
CLIP 1 - Extreme Wide/Drone: ├── Massive scale - landscape, city, era ├── No characters
OR tiny distant figures ├── Pure atmosphere and world ├── Camera: Slow drone/crane,
sweeping
CLIP 2 - Closer Wide: ├── More detail of location ├── Specific area/building in focus ├──
Maybe hint of activity ├── Camera: Slow push-in or tracking
CLIP 3 - Medium Context: ├── Entrance to main location ├── OR approach to main
character ├── Environment details clear ├── Camera: Deliberate, measured movement
CLIP 4 - Character Introduction: ├── Main character first appearance ├── Don’t show face
immediately (mystery) ├── Hands, back, silhouette first ├── Camera: Following or
revealing
CLIP 5 - Character Reveal: ├── Face visible for first time ├── Expression sets tone ├──
Held for recognition ├── Camera: Close or medium-close


### ⚠️ OPENING - LASTFRAME HANDLING:

OPENING SEQUENCE LASTFRAME RULES:
CLIPS 1-2: NEW IMAGE (Different scales) ├── Extreme wide → Wide requires new
composition ├── Drone shot → Ground shot requires new image
CLIPS 2-3: CONSIDER LASTFRAME ├── If continuous push-in toward location ├── Similar
framing continuing
CLIPS 3-4-5: MOSTLY NEW IMAGE ├── Different subjects (location → character) ├──
Different framings ├── Character reveal needs specific composition
GENERAL RULE FOR OPENINGS: ├── 80% NEW IMAGE ├── 20% LASTFRAME (only for
continuous camera moves)

---

## TYPE H: TRANSITION/BREATHING SEQUENCES 😮💨

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ HOLLYWOOD RULE: Breathing room = RECOVERY for audience ║ ║ ║ ║
After intensity, let them rest. Process what happened. ║ ║ Slower pace, minimal action,
emotional landing. ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝

### BREATHING ROOM STRUCTURE:
AFTER INTENSE SEQUENCE, INSERT 1-2 CLIPS:
BREATHING CLIP OPTION A - Environment: ├── Wide shot of location ├── No character
action ├── Just atmosphere, settling ├── Camera: Static or very slow drift
BREATHING CLIP OPTION B - Character Processing: ├── Close on face ├── No dialogue
├── Just breathing, thinking ├── Camera: Static, intimate
BREATHING CLIP OPTION C - Detail Focus: ├── Meaningful object ├── Related to what
just happened ├── Symbolic or plot-relevant ├── Camera: Slow push or static
PLACEMENT RULE: ├── After 3-4 intense clips: Insert 1 breathing clip ├── After major
action sequence: Insert 2 breathing clips ├── After emotional peak: Insert 1-2 breathing
clips ├── Before new sequence: Breathing helps transition

---

## 📊 MASTER SCENE TYPE REFERENCE TABLE

╔════════════════╦═══════════════╦═══════════════╦═════
══════════╗ ║ SCENE TYPE ║ MOVEMENT ║ LASTFRAME ║ NEW IMAGE ║
╠════════════════╬═══════════════╬═══════════════╬═════
══════════╣ ║ ACTION/CHASE ║ Maximum ║ Rarely ║ 80-90% ║ ║ SUSPENSE ║
Slow→Fast ║ Buildup OK ║ 50-60% ║ ║ EXPLORATION ║ Measured ║ Same shot OK ║ 60-
70% ║ ║ DIALOGUE ║ Minimal ║ Same angle OK ║ Reverse=New ║ ║ EMOTIONAL ║
Minimal ║ Usually OK ║ 30-40% ║ ║ DESTRUCTION ║ Maximum ║ Rarely ║ 90%+ ║ ║
OPENING ║ Slow+Grand ║ Rarely ║ 80%+ ║ ║ BREATHING ║ Minimal ║ Usually OK ║ 40-
50% ║
╚════════════════╩═══════════════╩═══════════════╩═════
══════════╝

---

## 🔄 THE UNIVERSAL SHOT CYCLE

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ ║ ║ WIDE → MEDIUM → CLOSE → WIDE (repeat) ║ ║ ║ ║ This cycle
applies to ALL scene types! ║ ║ Adjust SPEED of cycle based on scene type: ║ ║ ║ ║
ACTION: Cycle every 2-3 clips ║ ║ NORMAL: Cycle every 4-5 clips ║ ║ SLOW: Cycle every
6-8 clips ║ ║ ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝
THE CYCLE EXPLAINED:
WIDE (Establishing): ├── Shows WHERE characters are ├── Shows SCALE of
environment ├── Gives context and geography ├── Use for: Opening shots, after major
action, scene transitions
MEDIUM (Action): ├── Shows characters from waist up ├── Body language visible ├──
Action details visible ├── Use for: Most dialogue, character movement
CLOSE (Emotion): ├── Face fills significant frame area ├── Eyes, expressions clear ├──
Maximum emotional connection ├── Use for: Reactions, realizations, key dialogue
THEN BACK TO WIDE for new context/cycle

---

## ⚡ REACTION SHOTS - NON-NEGOTIABLE RULE

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ ║ ║ 🚨 EVERY ACTION NEEDS A REACTION SHOT! 🚨 ║ ║ ║ ║
Blockbusters spend 30-40% of screen time on FACES ║ ║ reacting to events. Without
reactions, audience ║ ║ doesn’t know how to FEEL about what happened. ║ ║ ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝
REACTION PLACEMENT:
BEFORE major action: Show anticipation ├── Character sees what’s coming ├── Fear,
determination, preparation ├── Audience shares their perspective
DURING action: Show effort/emotion ├── Split second expressions ├── Pain, struggle,
focus ├── Keeps human stakes visible
AFTER major action: Show result emotionally ├── Relief, shock, grief, triumph ├──
Processing what happened ├── Audience processes WITH character
EXAMPLE SEQUENCE: ├── Clip 1: Character sees danger approaching (ANTICIPATION)
├── Clip 2: Action happens (EVENT) ├── Clip 3: Character’s face during (EFFORT) ├──
Clip 4: Result of action (OUTCOME) ├── Clip 5: Character reacts to result (PROCESSING)

---

## 🎵 PACING RHYTHM RULE

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ ║ ║ INTENSITY CYCLE - NEVER BREAK THIS PATTERN! ║ ║ ║ ║ BUILD
(4-5 clips) → PEAK (2-3 clips) → BREATHE (1-2) ║ ║ BUILD (4-5 clips) → PEAK (2-3 clips)
→ BREATHE (1-2) ║ ║ ║ ║ Each BUILD should be slightly more intense than last ║ ║ Until
CLIMAX (maximum peak, longest breathe after) ║ ║ ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝
NEVER: ├── 10+ clips of continuous high intensity (exhausting) ├── 5+ clips of
continuous low intensity (boring) ├── Peak without buildup (feels unearned) ├── Peak
without breathe (no release, no impact)
ALWAYS: ├── Earn every peak with proper build ├── Release every peak with breathing
room ├── Vary intensity throughout video ├── Biggest breathe after biggest peak

---

## 🪑 DIRECTION & SPATIAL ORIENTATION (CRITICAL!)

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ ║ ║ 🚨 #1 CAUSE OF “WRONG DIRECTION” IMAGES: 🚨 ║ ║ ║ ║ AI
doesn’t know your STORY INTENT! ║ ║ ║ ║ You THINK: “Soldiers approaching cave” ║ ║
You WRITE: “Soldiers on path near cave” ║ ║ AI GENERATES: Soldiers facing AWAY (could
be leaving!) ║ ║ ║ ║ SOLUTION: Explicitly describe FACING + MOVING direction! ║ ║ ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝

### THE DIRECTION PROBLEM EXPLAINED:

YOUR STORY INTENT: “Dogra soldiers are approaching the cave to capture the scholars”
YOUR PROMPT (WRONG): “12 soldiers with torches on mountain path, 60 feet from cave”

WHAT AI SEES: ├── 12 soldiers ✓ ├── Torches ✓ ├── Mountain path ✓ ├── 60 feet
from cave ✓ ├── Direction? ❌ NOT SPECIFIED! └── AI GUESSES → 50% chance of wrong
direction!
RESULT: Soldiers look like they’re LEAVING, not APPROACHING!

### DIRECTION SPECIFICATION RULES:

FOR EVERY MOVING SUBJECT, SPECIFY ALL THREE:
  1.   FACING DIRECTION: ├── “Soldiers FACING the cave mouth” ├── “Character
       FACING camera” ├── “Man FACING away from camera” └── “Group FACING left
       side of frame”

  2.   MOVEMENT DIRECTION: ├── “Marching TOWARD the cave entrance” ├──
       “Running AWAY FROM the explosion” ├── “Walking INTO the room” └──
       “Approaching FROM the right side”
  3.   ORIENTATION TO KEY POINT: ├── “Lead soldier CLOSEST to cave” ├── “Front of
       group NEAREST to camera” ├── “Character positioned BETWEEN camera and
       door” └── “Line extending AWAY from the cave down mountain”


### CAMERA + SUBJECT = WHAT WE SEE:

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ ║ ║ THINK: “From THIS camera position, what SIDE of the ║ ║ subject is
visible?” ║ ║ ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝
SCENARIO: Camera inside cave, soldiers outside approaching
WRONG ASSUMPTION: ├── Camera inside looking out ├── Soldiers climbing toward cave
└── We see their FRONTS? NO!
REALITY CHECK: ├── If soldiers are BETWEEN camera and the mountain behind them
├── And they’re facing UP toward camera/cave ├── Then YES, we see their FRONTS ✓
BUT IF: ├── Soldiers are on a path that goes PAST the cave ├── They might be facing
SIDEWAYS ├── We’d see their PROFILES
OR IF: ├── Soldiers already PASSED the cave view ├── Now climbing higher ├── We’d
see their BACKS!
SOLUTION: Be EXPLICIT about ALL of this!


### ❌ vs ✅ DIRECTION EXAMPLES:

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════ ❌ WRONG - Ambiguous Direction:
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════
“Wide shot from inside cave looking out. 12 Dogra soldiers with torches on the mountain
path, approximately 60 feet from the cave entrance, in single-file formation.”
PROBLEMS: ├── Which way are soldiers FACING? Unknown! ├── Are they coming or
going? Unknown! ├── Which end of line is closest to cave? Unknown! ├── What side of
soldiers do we SEE? Unknown! └── Result: AI generates random direction!
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════ ✅ CORRECT - Explicit Direction:
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════
“Wide shot from inside cave looking out through the cave mouth.
SUBJECTS - DIRECTION EXPLICIT: Exactly 12 Dogra soldiers with 12 torches are visible
OUTSIDE on the mountain path, approximately 60 feet from cave entrance.
FACING: Soldiers are FACING the cave mouth - their torch-lit FRONTS are oriented toward
the camera/cave. We can see the orange glow illuminating the FRONT of their bodies and
faces (silhouetted).
MOVEMENT: They are marching TOWARD the cave entrance, climbing UP the path that
leads directly into THIS cave.
FORMATION: Single-file line. The LEAD soldier (at HEAD of line) is CLOSEST to the cave
entrance - he is the nearest figure to camera. The line extends BEHIND him, going DOWN
and AWAY from the cave, with the last soldier being farthest from cave.
VISUAL RESULT: From camera’s POV inside cave, we see soldiers’ FRONTS approaching us.
Lead soldier largest, others progressively smaller as line recedes into distance.“
WHAT’S BETTER: ├── FACING explicitly stated (toward cave) ├── MOVEMENT explicitly
stated (toward cave, climbing up) ├── FORMATION orientation clear (lead closest, line
behind) ├── VISUAL RESULT described (what camera actually sees) └── ZERO ambiguity
= correct direction every time!

### DIRECTION CHECKLIST (Use for EVERY prompt with moving subjects):

☐ FACING: Which direction is subject’s FRONT pointing? “facing the cave” / “facing
camera” / “facing left”

☐ MOVING: Which direction is subject traveling? “marching toward” / “running away from”
/ “walking into”

☐ RELATIVE POSITION: Where is subject relative to key point? “closest to cave” / “between
camera and door” / “nearest the edge”

☐ FORMATION (if group): Which end is front, which is back? “lead soldier closest” / “front
of group at right” / “line extends behind”

☐ CAMERA SEES: From this camera angle, what side is visible? “fronts visible” / “backs to
camera” / “profile view”

### COMMON DIRECTION SCENARIOS:

SCENARIO 1: Soldiers approaching cave (threat coming) ├── Camera: Inside cave looking
out ├── Soldiers: FACING cave, FRONTS toward camera ├── Formation: Lead soldier
CLOSEST to cave entrance ├── Movement: Climbing UP path TOWARD cave ├── We see:
Their FRONTS, getting larger as they approach
SCENARIO 2: Character running away from danger ├── Camera: Behind the character ├──
Character: FACING AWAY from camera ├── Movement: Running TOWARD
foreground/distance ├── We see: Their BACK, getting smaller
SCENARIO 3: Two characters facing each other ├── Camera: From the side ├── Character
A: FACING RIGHT (toward Character B) ├── Character B: FACING LEFT (toward Character
A) ├── We see: PROFILES of both
SCENARIO 4: Character entering room ├── Camera: Inside room ├── Character: At
doorway, FACING INTO room (toward camera) ├── Movement: Stepping FORWARD into
room ├── We see: Their FRONT as they enter
SCENARIO 5: Chase sequence (pursuer behind target) ├── Camera: Looking at both ├──
Target: In foreground, FACING AWAY, running ├── Pursuer: Behind target, FACING same
direction, chasing ├── We see: BACKS of both, pursuer closing distance

### DIRECTION WORDS REFERENCE:

TOWARD words: approaching, coming, advancing, heading to, marching to AWAY words:
leaving, retreating, fleeing, receding, departing FACING words: oriented toward, fronts
visible, looking at, turned to BACK words: backs to camera, facing away, turned from
FORMATION words: ├── “Lead/front at [location]” ├── “Line extends from X to Y” ├──
“Nearest to / Farthest from” ├── “Head of formation at” ├── “Tail of formation at”

---

## ⭐ ULTIMATE PROMPT TEMPLATES (COPY-PASTE READY)

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ ║ ║ 🏆 THESE TEMPLATES APPLY ALL AI PSYCHOLOGY PRINCIPLES! ║
║ ║ ║ Use these EXACT structures for maximum effectiveness. ║ ║ Fill in the [brackets]
with your specific details. ║ ║ ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝


### 📸 ULTIMATE IMAGE PROMPT TEMPLATE:

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════ [TIME PERIOD] [LOCATION], [TIME OF DAY].
FRAMING: [Shot type] shot. Camera positioned [distance] from subject. [What percentage]
of frame filled by [main element].
SUBJECT - EXACTLY [NUMBER] [SUBJECT TYPE]: • Position: [LEFT/CENTER/RIGHT] of
frame, [distance] from camera • Facing: [DIRECTION] - their [FRONT/BACK/PROFILE]
toward camera • Appearance: [Detailed description with colors and materials] • Current
state: [What they’re doing in this frozen moment]
[If character, FULL DESCRIPTION:] • FACE: [Beard/no beard, length, color], [skin tone],
[expression] • HEAD: [Hair/cap type], [color], [position on head] • CLOTHING: [Outer layer
- color, material], [inner layer - color] • POSTURE: [Standing/sitting/running], leaning [X
degrees], [stance] • HANDS: [LEFT hand doing X], [RIGHT hand doing Y], [holding what]
KEY OBJECT (if any): • [Object name]: [size], [color], [material], [state - open/closed] •
Position: [Where relative to character/frame] • Visibility: [What parts visible - clasp, cover,
etc.]
ENVIRONMENT: • Location: [Specific place - not just “cave” but “narrow rocky alcove”] •
Background: [What’s behind subject - specific details] • Foreground: [What’s in front, if
anything] • Atmosphere: CRYSTAL CLEAR air, [weather if any]
LIGHTING - EXACT PERCENTAGES: • Primary: [Source] from [DIRECTION] ([XX]%) •
Secondary: [Source] from [DIRECTION] ([XX]%) • On subject: [Which parts lit], [which
parts in shadow] • Mood: [High contrast / soft / dramatic]
TECHNICAL: [aspect ratio], photorealistic, cinematic, ultra-detailed.
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════
WORD COUNT TARGET: 150-200 words minimum!


### 🎬 ULTIMATE VIDEO PROMPT TEMPLATE (v6.0 ENHANCED):

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════ 🎬 VIDEO PROMPT - Scene [X], Clip [X.X] ([SHOT TYPE]) Word Count:
[XXX] words ✅
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════
[HOOK LINE - State the MOST CRITICAL element first] “[Subject] [doing what], [critical
constraint].”
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── 📍 SCENE CONTEXT (Ground to attached image):
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── The attached image shows [describe: character position, appearance, environment,
lighting, shot type]. This is [Frame X of this scene].
Shot type: [EXTREME WIDE/WIDE/MEDIUM/CLOSE-UP/ECU] Motion rules for this shot:
[Large movements OK / Subtle only / Micro only]
Color Passport: Matches Scene [X] exactly - [temperature], [contrast].
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── 🎬 ACTION (6 seconds breakdown):
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
─────
SECONDS 0-2: [Opening state and first action] ├── [Subject] [specific action with EXACT
distance in inches/feet] ├── [Which body part - LEFT/RIGHT hand, direction] └── [What
other body parts do meanwhile]
SECONDS 2-4: [Development and progression]
├── [Next action with specific measurements] ├── [Direction -
TOWARD/AWAY/LEFT/RIGHT, speed] └── [Environmental motion if any]
SECONDS 4-6: [Conclusion approaching end state] ├── [Final action completing] ├──
[End position approaching] └── [Final expression settling]
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── 🎭 CHARACTER ACTING - [Name] (ALL 7 LAYERS REQUIRED!):
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
─────
LAYER 1 - EYES: • Direction: [Where looking - camera/ground/left 15°/distant] • State:
[Wide/narrowed/squinting/half-closed] • Moisture: [Dry/glistening/tears forming] •
Movement: [Static/darting/shifting - degrees and timing] → Shift: [Start direction] → [End
direction] at [X sec]
LAYER 2 - EYEBROWS: • Position: [Raised/lowered/furrowed/asymmetric] • Tension:
[Relaxed/tense/twitching] • Movement: [Static/slowly raising/quick furrow]
LAYER 3 - MOUTH & JAW: • Lips: [Parted/pressed/trembling/bitten] • Corners:
[Up/down/neutral/twitching] • Jaw: [Clenched/relaxed/dropped/grinding] • Breathing:
[Visible through mouth? Rate?]
LAYER 4 - HEAD & NECK: • Tilt: [Straight/left X°/right X°/forward/back] • Turn: [Rotation
degree toward what] • Neck: [Tendons visible/relaxed/straining] • Movement:
[Still/nodding/turning at X sec]
LAYER 5 - SHOULDERS: • Position: [Raised (tension)/dropped (defeat)/squared
(confidence)] • Tension: [Relaxed/tight/asymmetric] • Movement:
[Rising/falling/shrugging at X sec]
LAYER 6 - POSTURE & BREATHING: • Stance: [Standing/sitting/crouching] + lean
[direction X°] • Weight: [Centered/shifted left/forward] • Breathing: [Rate -
slow/normal/rapid], [depth], visible in [chest/shoulders] • Overall tension:
[Relaxed/alert/rigid]
LAYER 7 - HANDS (BOTH required!): • LEFT HAND: ├── Position: [Exact location - at
side/chest/holding X] ├── Fingers: [Spread/curled/gripping/pointing] ├── Action: [What
doing - holding/reaching/gesturing] └── State: [Steady/trembling/tense] • RIGHT HAND:
├── Position: [Exact location] ├── Fingers: [State] ├── Action: [What doing] └── State:
[Steady/trembling/tense]
EMOTIONAL LAYERS: • External (showing): [What they display to others] • Internal
(feeling): [True emotion underneath] • Intention: [What they want right now]

🔸 MICRO-EXPRESSION (At least 1 required!): • At [X] seconds: “[Brief involuntary tell]”
for [0.X sec] Then: [Recovery or change] Example: “At 3 sec, jaw muscle twitches once
(0.5s) before relaxing”
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── 🔒 CHARACTER LOCK:
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── • FACE: [Specific features - beard type/length, skin tone, structure] • HAIR: [Style,
color, current state] • CLOTHING: [EXACT items with COLORS - “olive green bomber
jacket”] • ACCESSORIES: [Watch, jewelry with specifics] → MUST remain IDENTICAL
throughout - NO morphing allowed!
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── 🔒 SCENE LOCKS (Stated POSITIVELY):
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── • Frame contains ONLY: [List exactly what’s allowed] • People count: EXACTLY [X]
- no additions, no removals • [Object] remains [STATE - OPEN/CLOSED/etc] throughout -
LOCKED • Lighting MAINTAINS: [Primary] from [direction] at [%], [Secondary] from
[direction] at [%] • Color grade: Per Scene [X] Color Passport - no drift • Single continuous
shot - no cuts, no fades, no transitions
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── 🎯 ENDS WITH (AI reads LAST - critical for continuity!):
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── At exactly 6 seconds, frame shows: • Subject position: [EXACT - X feet from camera,
frame LEFT/CENTER/RIGHT] • Subject expression: [Specific final expression state] •
Subject posture: [Final body position, lean, stance] • Eyes: [Final direction and state] •
Hands: [Where each hand ends - LEFT at X, RIGHT at Y] • Object states: [Each object’s final
state] • Camera frame: [Final composition]
→ This becomes START point for Clip [X.X+1] → Next clip MUST match these end positions
exactly!
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── ⚙️ TECHNICAL:
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── Duration: 6 seconds | Aspect: 16:9 | Style: Cinematic photorealistic
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════ WORD COUNT: [XXX] words (MINIMUM 150 required!)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════
### ⚠️ VIDEO PROMPT VERIFICATION CHECKLIST:

Before showing ANY video prompt to user, verify ALL:

☐ Word count 150+ words? ☐ Shot type specified with motion rules? ☐ All 7 acting layers
included? ☐ Both hands described separately? ☐ At least 1 micro-expression included? ☐
External + Internal emotion stated? ☐ Time breakdown (0-2s, 2-4s, 4-6s)? ☐ Distances in
feet/inches? ☐ Lighting in percentages? ☐ CHARACTER LOCK complete? ☐ ENDS WITH
section detailed? ☐ Previous clip’s ENDS WITH matches this START?

IF ANY ☐ IS EMPTY → REWRITE BEFORE SHOWING!


### ✅ FILLED EXAMPLE - IMAGE PROMPT (195 words):

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════ 1858 Kashmir, interior of a hidden rocky cave at night.
FRAMING: Medium-wide shot. Camera positioned 12 feet inside the cave, looking toward
entrance. Cave opening fills 45% of frame width, centered.
SUBJECT - EXACTLY 1 elderly scholar: • Position: CENTER of frame, 15 feet from camera •
Facing: TOWARD camera - his FRONT visible • Current state: Mid-stride, running toward
camera
FULL CHARACTER DESCRIPTION: • FACE: Grey-white full beard (3 inches), weathered
brown skin, fearful expression with wide eyes • HEAD: Patterned Kashmiri kufi cap
(cream/brown geometric), firmly on head • CLOTHING: Grey-brown woolen shawl (patu)
over dark brown kurta • POSTURE: Leaning forward 15 degrees, mid-running stride •
HANDS: BOTH hands clutch ancient book at sternum level, cover facing outward
KEY OBJECT - The Book: • Ancient leather-bound book, approximately 12x9 inches •
CLOSED - brass clasp visible on right edge, secured • Position: Pressed against chest at
sternum height
ENVIRONMENT: • Location: Rocky cave interior, rough grey-brown stone walls •
Background: Cave entrance showing dark blue night sky • Atmosphere: CRYSTAL CLEAR
air, no fog
LIGHTING - EXACT PERCENTAGES: • Primary: Warm orange lantern from LEFT (65%) •
Secondary: Cold blue moonlight from entrance BEHIND (35%) • On subject: Face 60% lit,
40% shadow
TECHNICAL: 16:9, photorealistic, cinematic, ultra-detailed.
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════
### ✅ FILLED EXAMPLE - VIDEO PROMPT (175 words):

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════ 🎬 VIDEO PROMPT - Clip 1.3
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════
ELDERLY SCHOLAR running TOWARD camera, CLUTCHING the CLOSED book.
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── SCENE CONTEXT:
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── The attached image shows a 72-year-old scholar mid-stride inside a dark rocky
cave. He clutches an ancient book against his chest. Warm lantern light from LEFT, cold
moonlight from BEHIND.
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── ACTION (6 seconds):
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── The scholar continues running TOWARD camera with urgent energy. His LEFT foot
completes its step, then RIGHT foot swings forward 18 inches, then LEFT again - three
strides covering 5 feet total. Body stays leaned forward 15 degrees throughout.
His hands MAINTAIN tight grip on the CLOSED book at sternum level. Cover faces outward,
brass clasp visible on RIGHT edge. Book stays LOCKED to chest, moving with body.
Final 2 seconds: Pace slows - last step only 10 inches. Posture straightens slightly, reaching
destination.
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── CHARACTER LOCK:
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── • FACE: Grey-white full beard, weathered brown skin, fearful eyes • HEAD:
Patterned Kashmiri cap, firmly positioned • CLOTHING: Grey-brown woolen shawl over
dark kurta → MUST remain CONSISTENT - NO morphing
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── LOCKS:
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── • Character appearance LOCKED as described • ONLY 1 person in frame - nothing
else appears • Book remains CLOSED throughout - CONFIRMED • Lighting MAINTAINS:
warm LEFT 65%, cool BEHIND 35%
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── ENDS WITH:
───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
───── • Scholar: 10 feet from camera (was 15), slightly more upright • Expression:
Fearful but determined • Book: STILL CLOSED at chest, clasp visible • Ready for next action

## ⚠️ MANDATORY: OPEN LOOPS & TRANSITIONS MAP

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ ║ ║ BEFORE generating prompts, YOU MUST create: ║ ║ ║ ║ 1. OPEN
LOOPS MAP - Which loop opens/closes in each scene ║ ║ 2. TRANSITIONS MAP - How each
scene connects to next ║ ║ ║ ║ This ensures retention and momentum throughout video!
║║║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝

### CREATE THIS TABLE FOR EVERY VIDEO:

Scene    Loop Opens           Loop Closes       Transition Hook
1        “Why 23 died?”       -                 “But he left a NISHANI…”
2        “What’s the code?”   Partial: book     “Whoever finds it…”
3        “Who’s following?”   “23 died” fully   “They’re not alone…”
4        …                    …                 …
Climax   -                    ALL remaining     Resolution

RULES: ├── Scene 1 MUST open at least 1 strong loop ├── Never close without opening
new (until climax) ├── Maximum 3 active loops at any time ├── Every scene ends with
TRANSITION HOOK ├── Climax closes ALL remaining loops

### EXAMPLE OPEN LOOPS MAP:

SCENE 1 (1858 - The Origin): ├── OPENS Loop A: “23 log kyun mare?” (Why did they die?)
├── OPENS Loop B: “Kitaab mein kya hai?” (What’s in the book?) └── TRANSITION: “Usne
ek nishani chhodi thi…”
SCENE 2 (Present - Discovery): ├── PARTIAL CLOSE A: “Kitaab mein codes the” (codes, but
what do they DO?) ├── OPENS Loop C: “Dada kyun gayab hue?” (Why did Dada
disappear?) └── TRANSITION: “Jab usne diary kholi…”
SCENE 3 (The Revelation): ├── CLOSES B: “Code ek location point karta hai” (Kashmir!)
├── OPENS Loop D: “Warning kya thi?” (What danger awaits?) └── TRANSITION: “Jo
wahan gaye, woh laute nahi…”
…continue for all scenes…
CLIMAX: ├── CLOSES C: Full Dada revelation ├── CLOSES D: Warning meaning revealed
└── RESOLUTION: Satisfying conclusion

---

## 🔀 SCENE TRANSITIONS - MAINTAINING MOMENTUM

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ ║ ║ BAD TRANSITIONS = Viewer loses interest between scenes ║ ║
GOOD TRANSITIONS = Viewer pulled INTO next scene ║ ║ ║ ║ The moment between
scenes is CRITICAL! ║ ║ ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝

### THE TRANSITION PROBLEM:

❌ BAD TRANSITION (Momentum Dies):
SCENE 1 ENDS: “Ibraheem ne kitaab chhupa di.” (Loop CLOSED. Story feels complete.)
SCENE 2 STARTS: “166 saal baad, Faisalabad mein ek ladka rehta tha…” (New topic. No
connection. Feels disconnected.)
PROBLEM: ├── Scene 1 loop fully closed = No reason to continue ├── Scene 2 starts fresh
= Feels like different video ├── No PULL into next scene ├── Energy DROPS between
scenes └── Viewer might click away here!

### THE SOLUTION: BRIDGE TRANSITIONS

✅ GOOD TRANSITION (Momentum Continues):
SCENE 1 ENDS: “Ibraheem ne kitaab chhupa di. Lekin marne se pehle, usne ek NISHANI
chhodi thi… apne khandaan ke liye.” (Loop partially closed + NEW LOOP opened!)
SCENE 2 STARTS: “166 saal baad. Faisalabad. Ibraheem ke parpoton ka parpota… Zain… usi
nishani ko dhundh raha tha.” (CONNECTS to Scene 1 loop!)
WHAT’S BETTER: ├── Scene 1 hints at what’s coming (nishani) ├── Scene 2 REFERENCES
Scene 1 mystery ├── Viewer wants to know: “Kya hai nishani?” ├── Smooth PULL from
past to present ├── Energy MAINTAINED between scenes └── Viewer MUST continue
watching!

### TRANSITION TECHNIQUES:
TECHNIQUE 1: THE HOOK FORWARD
═══════════════════════════════════════ End scene with hint about
NEXT scene:
“Ibraheem ne kitaab chhupa di. Lekin usne ek aur kaam kiya… jo 166 saal baad Zain ki
zindagi badal dega.”
→ Viewer thinks: “Kya kiya? Kaise badlega?” → MUST watch Scene 2!
TECHNIQUE 2: THE QUESTION BRIDGE
═══════════════════════════════════════ End with question,
answer begins next scene:
Scene 1 ends: “Lekin sawaal yeh tha - kya raaz 166 saal baad bhi safe rahega?”
Scene 2 starts: “166 saal guzar gaye. Aur raaz safe tha… jab tak Zain ne woh diary nahi
kholi.”
→ Question from Scene 1 answered in Scene 2 → Smooth connection!
TECHNIQUE 3: THE VISUAL MATCH
═══════════════════════════════════════ End and start on SIMILAR
visual:
Scene 1 ends: Close-up of OLD book being hidden (1858) Scene 2 starts: Close-up of SAME
book being found (Present)
OR
Scene 1 ends: Snow-covered mountains (Past) Scene 2 starts: Photo of same mountains on
wall (Present)
→ Visual LINK connects scenes!
TECHNIQUE 4: THE AUDIO BRIDGE
═══════════════════════════════════════ Narration CONTINUES
over scene change:
“Ibraheem ne apni jaan de di… [VISUAL: Scene changes from 1858 to Present] …taake aaj
Zain is kamre mein khada ho sake.”
→ Voice creates continuity over visual cut!
TECHNIQUE 5: THE CONTRAST BRIDGE
═══════════════════════════════════════ Deliberate contrast that
connects:
Scene 1 ends: “Us raat sirf ek CHEEKH suni gayi thi.” Scene 2 starts: “Aaj us haveli mein
KHAMOSHI hai.”
→ CHEEKH vs KHAMOSHI = contrast creates connection!
### SCENE TRANSITION CHECKLIST:

☐ Does Scene end with FORWARD HOOK? (Hint about next) ☐ Does Scene end with OPEN
LOOP? (Unanswered question) ☐ Does next Scene REFERENCE previous scene? ☐ Is there
VISUAL or AUDIO bridge? ☐ Does energy MAINTAIN across transition? ☐ Would viewer
feel PULL into next scene?
If ANY answer is NO → REWRITE TRANSITION!

### EXAMPLE: FULL TRANSITION MAPPING

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════ SCENE 1 → SCENE 2 TRANSITION:
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════ END SCENE 1: “Ibraheem ne kitaab chhupa di. Aur apne khoon se ek
NISHANI likhi… jo sirf uska khandaan samajh sake.” (FORWARD HOOK: What nishani?
What does it mean?)
START SCENE 2: “166 saal baad. Woh NISHANI ek purani diary mein mili - Zain ke haathon
mein.” (REFERENCES Scene 1 hook!)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════ SCENE 2 → SCENE 3 TRANSITION:
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════ END SCENE 2: “Zain ne cipher solve kar liya. Answer ek location thi -
Kashmir mein. Lekin wahan jane ka matlab…” (OPEN LOOP: Kya matlab? Danger hint!)
START SCENE 3: “…487 kilometer. Ek purani bike pe. Do dost. Aur ek raasta jahan se pichle
3 log laute nahi.” (ANSWERS partial + raises stakes!)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════ SCENE 5 → SCENE 6 TRANSITION:
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════ END SCENE 5: [VISUAL: Zain finds hidden compartment] NARRATION: “Aur
jab Zain ne woh darwaza khola…” (CLIFFHANGER + AUDIO continues over cut)
START SCENE 6: [VISUAL: What’s inside compartment] NARRATION: “…uski duniya badal
gayi.” (AUDIO BRIDGE complete!)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════

---






# PHASE 5: PROMPT GENERATION

## 🚨🚨🚨 MANDATORY: SCENE PREP PACKAGE (Before ANY Clips!) 🚨🚨🚨

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ ║ ║ ⛔ STOP! Before generating ANY clip prompts for a scene, ║ ║ you
MUST complete the SCENE PREP PACKAGE first! ║ ║ ║ ║ This is NOT optional. Skipping
this = Amateur output! ║ ║ ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝

### SCENE PREP PACKAGE TEMPLATE (Complete for EVERY scene):

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════ 📋 SCENE [X] PREP PACKAGE — “[Scene Name]”
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════
┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ ┃ 🎨 SCENE COLOR PASSPORT
┃
┣━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┫ ┃ Time of day: [Specific - e.g.,
“Late afternoon, 4:30 PM”] ┃ ┃ Color temperature: [Kelvin - e.g., “~5200K neutral-warm”]
┃ ┃ Shadows: [Hex + desc - e.g., “soft charcoal (#2a2a2a)”] ┃ ┃ Midtones: [Description -
e.g., “warm beige skin tones”] ┃ ┃ Highlights: [Hex + desc - e.g., “warm-white (#f0e7da)”]
┃ ┃ Saturation: [Percentage - e.g., “55%”] ┃ ┃ Contrast: [Percentage - e.g., “55%”] ┃ ┃
Exposure: [Stops - e.g., “0 to -0.2 stop”] ┃ ┃ Atmosphere: [e.g., “clean air, no haze” or “light
fog”] ┃ ┃ ┃ ┃ CONTINUITY COMMAND (include in every prompt this scene): ┃ ┃
“Color grading MUST match Scene [X] Color Passport exactly. ┃ ┃ Zero tolerance for color
drift.” ┃
┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛

┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ ┃ 🎵 SCENE AUDIO PLAN ┃
┣━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┫ ┃ ┃ ┃ 🎶 BG MUSIC (ONE track
for scene/section): ┃ ┃ ├── Clips covered: [X.1] – [X.X] (continuous) ┃ ┃ ├── Track:
“[Exact track name]” ┃ ┃ ├── Artist: “[Artist name]” ┃ ┃ ├── Source: [Mixkit/Pixabay
URL] ┃ ┃ ├── Mood/Genre: [e.g., “Suspense, building tension”] ┃ ┃ └── Notes: [Fade
in/out instructions, volume notes] ┃ ┃ ┃ ┃ 🎙️ NARRATION (ONE continuous recording,
NOT per clip!): ┃ ┃ ├── Clips covered: [X.X] – [X.X] ([X] seconds total) ┃ ┃ ├── Clips
SILENT (no narration): [List them] ┃ ┃ │ ┃ ┃ ├── FULL TEXT (Single continuous take):
┃ ┃ │ “[Write complete narration in Roman Urdu or English]” ┃ ┃ │ ┃ ┃ ├──
NARRATION TYPE: [Atmospheric/Informational/Emotional/ ┃ ┃ │ Tension/Reflective]
┃ ┃ │ ┃ ┃ └── VOICE DIRECTION: ┃ ┃ ├── Voice: [Age, gender, tone - “Male, 40s,
deep”] ┃ ┃ ├── Pace: [Slow/Medium/Fast/Varied] ┃ ┃ ├── Tone:
[Whispered/Conversational/Intense/Grave] ┃ ┃ ├── Emotion:
[Curious/Fearful/Reverent/Urgent] ┃ ┃ ├── Pauses: [Where and how long - “1.5s after
‘tha’”] ┃ ┃ └── Emphasis: [Words to stress - in CAPS] ┃ ┃ ┃ ┃ 🗣️ DIALOGUES (Only
clips where characters speak): ┃ ┃ ├── Clip [X.X]: [Character]: “[Line]” ┃ ┃ │ └──
Delivery: [Whispered/Normal], [Emotion] ┃ ┃ ├── Clip [X.X]: [Character]: “[Line]” ┃ ┃
│ └── Delivery: [Tone], [Emotion] ┃ ┃ └── (Continue for all dialogue clips) ┃ ┃ ┃ ┃
🔊 SOUND EFFECTS (Per clip where needed): ┃ ┃ ├── Clip [X.1]: “[SFX name]” - [Source]
(0:XX-0:XX) ┃ ┃ ├── Clip [X.2]: “[SFX name]” - [Source] (timing) ┃ ┃ ├── Clip [X.3]:
NONE ┃ ┃ └── (Continue for all clips, marking NONE where not needed) ┃ ┃ ┃ ┃
NARRATION QUALITY CHECK (must pass ALL): ┃ ┃ ☐ Has specific NAMES? (not “ek
shakhs”) ┃ ┃ ☐ Has specific NUMBERS? (not “kaafi saal”) ┃ ┃ ☐ Creates QUESTIONS in
viewer mind? ┃ ┃ ☐ Has STAKES/consequences? ┃ ┃ ☐ FREE of excessive “…” pauses?
┃ ┃ ☐ Is DRAMATIC not DRY? ┃
┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛

┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ ┃ 📈 SCENE EMOTIONAL ARC ┃
┣━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┫ ┃ EMOTION LEVELS: 1=Mild,
2=Medium, 3=Strong, 4=Intense, 5=Peak┃ ┃ ┃ ┃ Clip-by-clip arc: ┃ ┃ ├── Clips [X.1-
X.3]: [Emotion name] (Level [X]) ┃ ┃ ├── Clips [X.4-X.6]: [Emotion name] (Level [X]) ┃
┃ ├── Clips [X.7-X.9]: [Emotion name] (Level [X]) ┃ ┃ └── Clips [X.10-X.12]: [Emotion
name] (Level [X]) ┃ ┃ ┃ ┃ DOMINANT EMOTION: [What viewer should primarily feel]
┃ ┃ CONTRAST MOMENT: [Brief opposite] at Clip [X.X] ┃ ┃ PEAK MOMENT: [Highest
point] at Clip [X.X] ┃
┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛

┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ ┃ 🔄 OPEN LOOPS STATUS ┃
┣━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┫ ┃ COMING INTO THIS SCENE: ┃
┃ ├── Loop A: “[Question]” - Status: [Open/Partial] ┃ ┃ ├── Loop B: “[Question]” -
Status: [Open/Partial] ┃ ┃ └── Loop C: “[Question]” - Status: [Open] ┃ ┃ ┃ ┃ THIS
SCENE OPENS: ┃ ┃ ├── Loop [X]: “[New question raised]” ┃ ┃ └── Loop [X]: “[New
question raised]” ┃ ┃ ┃ ┃ THIS SCENE CLOSES/ADVANCES: ┃ ┃ ├── Loop [X]: [Closes
with answer / Partial clue] ┃ ┃ └── Loop [X]: [Advances how] ┃ ┃ ┃ ┃ TRANSITION
HOOK TO NEXT SCENE: ┃ ┃ “[The line or visual that pulls viewer to next scene]” ┃
┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════ ✅ SCENE PREP COMPLETE — Now proceed to clip prompts
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════


### ⛔ CRITICAL RULES FOR SCENE PREP:

  1.   COMPLETE ALL 4 SECTIONS before ANY clip prompt!
  2.   Audio Plan covers ENTIRE scene, not per-clip!
  3.   40-60% of clips should be SILENT (no narration)
  4.   BG Music = ONE track for 3-6+ clips minimum
  5.   Narration = ONE continuous recording
  6.   Only SFX and Dialogues are per-clip
  7.   Color Passport command goes in EVERY clip prompt!

---

## ⚠️⚠️⚠️ CRITICAL: IMAGE vs LASTFRAME DECISION ⚠️⚠️⚠️

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ ║ ║ DEFAULT = NEW IMAGE (with character/object references!) ║ ║
LASTFRAME = ONLY for CONTINUOUS shots! ║ ║ ║ ║ Lastframe is EXCEPTION, not
DEFAULT! ║ ║ ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝


### ❌ WRONG UNDERSTANDING (What AI thinks):

“Every clip after first = use lastframe”

Clip 1.1: Image ✓ Clip 1.2: Lastframe Clip 1.3: Lastframe Clip 1.4: Lastframe …
This is WRONG! This makes sense only for CONTINUOUS shots!


### ✅ CORRECT UNDERSTANDING:

MOST clips = NEW IMAGE (with references attached!) ONLY truly continuous shots =
Lastframe
Clip 1.1: NEW IMAGE (wide shot haveli) Clip 1.2: NEW IMAGE (Zain walking - attach
CHAR_Zain_ref!) Clip 1.3: NEW IMAGE (close-up door - new angle!) Clip 1.4: LASTFRAME
(door opening - CONTINUES from 1.3) Clip 1.5: NEW IMAGE (inside room - new location!)
Clip 1.6: NEW IMAGE (Zain’s face reaction - attach CHAR_Zain_ref!) Clip 1.7: LASTFRAME
(Zain walks forward - CONTINUES from 1.6)

---

### WHEN TO USE WHAT:

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════ 📸 USE NEW IMAGE (Most clips - 70-80%):
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════

✓ Different camera angle than previous clip ✓ Different shot type (wide → medium →
close-up) ✓ Character appears who wasn’t in previous frame ✓ New location or
significantly different framing ✓ Object appears that wasn’t visible before ✓ Any cut/edit
point in cinematic terms
ALWAYS ATTACH REFERENCES: ├── Character visible? → Attach CHAR_[Name]ref.png
├── Specific object? → Attach OBJ[Name]_ref.png ├── Multiple characters? → Attach ALL
character refs! └── NO EXCEPTIONS!
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════ 🔗 USE LASTFRAME (Only continuous shots - 20-30%):
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════

✓ SAME camera angle continuing ✓ SAME framing, just action progressing ✓ Character
doing continuous motion (walking, reaching) ✓ Door opening that started in previous clip
✓ Smooth camera movement continuing
Examples of CONTINUOUS shots: ├── Clip 3.4: Zain reaches for book ├── Clip 3.5:
LASTFRAME - Hand grabs book (continues reach) │ ├── Clip 5.2: Door starts opening ├──
Clip 5.3: LASTFRAME - Door fully opens (continues motion) │ ├── Clip 7.1: Zain walking
down corridor ├── Clip 7.2: LASTFRAME - Continues walking (same shot)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════

---

### SCENE EXAMPLE - Correct Approach:

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════ SCENE 3: Zain Discovers the Book (6 clips)
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════
Clip 3.1: Wide shot - study room ├── TYPE: NEW IMAGE ├── ATTACH: None
(environment only) └── WHY: Establishing shot, new angle
Clip 3.2: Medium shot - Zain enters room ├── TYPE: NEW IMAGE ← Not lastframe! ├──
ATTACH: CHAR_[Hero]_Protagonist_ref.png └── WHY: Character appears, different framing
Clip 3.3: Close-up - Zain’s face looking around ├── TYPE: NEW IMAGE ← Not lastframe!
├── ATTACH: CHAR_[Hero]_Protagonist_ref.png └── WHY: Different shot type (medium →
close-up)
Clip 3.4: Medium shot - Zain approaches bookshelf ├── TYPE: NEW IMAGE ← Not
lastframe! ├── ATTACH: CHAR_[Hero]_Protagonist_ref.png └── WHY: Different angle, new
framing

Clip 3.5: Close-up - Hand reaching for book ├── TYPE: LASTFRAME ✓ (Continues from 3.4)
├── ATTACH: Clip_3.4_lastframe.png └── WHY: CONTINUOUS action - reach continues

Clip 3.6: Extreme close-up - Book being pulled out ├── TYPE: LASTFRAME ✓ (Continues
from 3.5) ├── ATTACH: Clip_3.5_lastframe.png └── WHY: CONTINUOUS action - pull
continues
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
═════════

---

### SUMMARY:

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ ║ ║ 70-80% clips: NEW IMAGE (always attach references!) ║ ║ 20-30%
clips: LASTFRAME (only for continuous shots) ║ ║ ║ ║ When in doubt: USE NEW IMAGE
WITH REFERENCES! ║ ║ ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝

## 🔒 CHARACTER CONSISTENCY LOCK (CRITICAL FOR VIDEO!)

╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╗ ║ ║ ║ 🚨 #1 REASON FOR BAD AI VIDEO: CHARACTER MORPHING! 🚨 ║ ║
║ ║ If you don’t LOCK character details in video prompt, ║ ║ Grok will CHANGE face,
clothes, proportions mid-clip! ║ ║ ║ ║ EVERY video prompt with a character MUST have a
║ ║ CHARACTER LOCK section! ║ ║ ║
╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════╝

### CHARACTER LOCK CHECKLIST (Include in EVERY video prompt!):

CHARACTER LOCK FOR [Name]: ├── FACE: [beard type/length, skin tone, expression]
├── HEAD: [hair/cap description, color, position] ├── CLOTHING: [each visible layer with
color] ├── POSTURE: [body angle, stance] └── “MUST remain consistent throughout - no
morphing”

### WHY CHARACTER LOCK MATTERS:

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════ ❌ WITHOUT CHARACTER LOCK:
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════
VIDEO PROMPT: “The elderly man walks forward 3 steps while holding the book.”
WHAT HAPPENS: ├── Frame 1: Face clear, beard visible ├── Frame 30: Face darker,
features shifting ├── Frame 60: Beard shape different ├── Frame 90: Clothes color
slightly changed ├── Frame 120: Proportions distorted ├── Frame 144: Character looks
like different person! └── RESULT: Unusable “melting” video!
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════ ✅ WITH CHARACTER LOCK:
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════
════════
VIDEO PROMPT: “The elderly man walks forward 3 steps while holding the book.
CHARACTER LOCK:
  •   FACE: Grey-white full beard (3 inches), weathered brown skin, fearful expression
      with furrowed brow
  •   HEAD: Patterned Kashmiri cap (cream/brown geometric), firmly on head at slight
      forward tilt
  •   CLOTHING: Grey-brown woolen shawl over dark brown kurta
  •   POSTURE: Upright, leaning forward 10-15 degrees while walking
MUST remain consistent throughout - no morphing allowed.“
WHAT HAPPENS: ├── Frame 1: Character matches description ├── Frame 30: Character
still matches ├── Frame 60: Character still matches ├── Frame 90: Character still matches
├── Frame 144: Character STILL matches! └── RESULT: Consistent, professional video!

### CHARACTER LOCK TEMPLATES:
**For ELDERLY MALE:**

CHARACTER LOCK:
  •   FACE: [Full/trimmed] [color] beard ([X] inches), [skin tone] weathered skin,
      [expression - fearful/determined/calm]
  •   HEAD: [Cap type] with [pattern/color], positioned [firmly/tilted]
  •   CLOTHING: [Outer layer color/type] over [inner layer color/type]
  •   POSTURE: [Standing/walking/sitting], [body angle]
  •   MUST remain consistent throughout - no morphing

**For YOUNG MALE:**

CHARACTER LOCK:
  •   FACE: [Clean-shaven/stubble], [skin tone], [expression]
  •   HAIR: [Color], [style - short/medium], [any distinctive feature]
  •   CLOTHING: [Jacket/shirt type and color], [pants if visible]
  •   BUILD: [Slim/medium/athletic]
  •   POSTURE: [Stance, body angle]
  •   MUST remain consistent throughout - no morphing

**For FEMALE:**

CHARACTER LOCK:
  •   FACE: [Skin tone], [expression], [any distinctive features]
  •   HAIR/HEAD: [Covered/visible], [color if visible], [style]
  •   CLOTHING: [Outer garment type/color], [visible layers]
  •   POSTURE: [Stance, body angle]
  •   MUST remain consistent throughout - no morphing

## ⚠️ IMAGE REFERENCE RULES (STRICTLY FOLLOW):

**When generating NEW IMAGE, ALWAYS mention references to attach!**

📸 IMAGE - Clip 3.2

🔗 REFERENCES TO ATTACH:
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────┐ │
CHAR_[Hero]_Protagonist_ref.png │ │ (You created this at project start) │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
📝 PROMPT: “Using character from attached reference…”


---

### REFERENCE ATTACHMENT TABLE:

| Situation | What to Attach | Notes |
|-----------|----------------|-------|
| Environment only | None or existing image | No character refs needed |
| Character visible | CHAR_[Name]_ref.png | ALWAYS attach! |
| Multiple characters | ALL character refs | In order (1st=LEFT, 2nd=RIGHT) |
| Character + Object | Both refs | Character 1st, Object 2nd |
| Continuous shot | Previous lastframe | Only for truly continuous action |

---

❌ WRONG:

Attach: Character_reference.png (1st image), LastFrame.png (2nd image)


✅ CORRECT (for hand/body only):

Attach: Clip_X.X_lastframe.png (1st attached image)
Same environment from attached image. A man’s hand visible - medium brown skin,
wearing olive green cargo jacket sleeve…


✅ CORRECT (for face first appearance):

Generate NEW IMAGE with Gemini (Nano-Banana-Pro) that includes character in scene:
[Environment description from story] [Character NAME from character reference] (age,
skin tone, hair, clothing - copy from character reference) standing/sitting/walking in
[position]. [Expression and pose]. [Aspect ratio], photorealistic, cinematic lighting.
